## 3D and 4D seismic acquisition (towed streamer, ocean-bottom nodes)
<a id="ups-seismic-acquisition"></a>

Sound sources generate waves that reflect from subsurface layers and are recorded by receivers: towed streamers (5 to 15 m deep, Ramform vessels tow up to 24), ocean-bottom cables, or battery-powered ocean-bottom nodes placed by ROV. A 4D survey repeats a 3D survey after production starts to watch reservoir changes.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SRC["Generate seismic energy<br/><small>air-gun arrays 2,000 to 7,000 cubic inches, vibroseis on land</small>"]
    REC["Record reflections<br/><small>streamers, ocean-bottom nodes</small>"]
    PROC["Process into 3D volume; repeat for 4D<br/><small>processing cluster</small>"]
    SRC --> REC
    REC --> PROC
```

**Key equipment:** seismic vessel, air-gun arrays, streamers or nodes, ROV or node deployment, processing cluster.

**Performance:**
- towed-streamer depth: 5-15 m; marine streamer towing. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reflection_seismology)
- streamers towed by a Ramform vessel: 24 streamers (up to); spreads can exceed one nautical mile. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reflection_seismology)
- ocean-bottom node deployment depth: 3000 m (about, by ROV); 30-day battery example; node weight over 10 kg. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reflection_seismology)

**Missing inputs:**
- survey cost per km2 with year (costs): Not in the free source; broker and vendor price data are commercial. Where: Wood Mackenzie or Westwood survey cost reports (paid). Next: Add a published cost range with year.
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; survey trade press names campaigns but no verifiable per-survey figures on open pages. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched seismic, geophone, exploration, acquisition); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Basin and petroleum systems modelling
<a id="ups-basin-petroleum-systems-modelling"></a>

Software reconstructs burial, heat flow and fluid flow in a sedimentary basin to estimate when and where source rocks generated oil or gas, how it migrated and whether a trap was in place. The general framework is source rock, burial heat, migration, reservoir, seal and trap.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IN["Assemble geology, thermal history and kinetics<br/><small>basin model</small>"]
    SIM["Simulate burial, maturation and migration<br/><small>PetroMod or similar</small>"]
    OUT["Predict charge, timing and fluid type"]
    IN --> SIM
    SIM --> OUT
```

**Key equipment:** basin modelling software, geochemical kinetic data, seismic-derived structure.

**Performance:**
- oil generation depth range: 1000-6000 m; about 60 to 150 °C. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_system)
- model dimensions documented for petroleum systems modeling workflows (PetroMod): 1-3 D (model dimensionality); USGS OFR 2006-1024 documents the PetroMod workflow (1D to 3D) with calibration data from source-rock pyrolysis (kerogen type, TOC, HI, kinetics) and well thermal data; SLB documents heat flow calibration to well temperature and vitrinite reflectance and the PetroRisk uncertainty module; example result: migration flowpaths and accumulations modeled for the Mannville petroleum system, Western Canada Sedimentary Basin. [source](https://pubs.usgs.gov/of/2006/1024/pdf/OFR-2006-1024.pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- USGS Western Canada Sedimentary Basin petroleum systems modeling demonstration (PetroMod), CA: 1D, 2D and 3D petroleum systems models of the Lower Cretaceous Mannville petroleum system, Western Canada Sedimentary Basin, applied to USGS oil and gas resource assessment; pilot. [source](https://pubs.usgs.gov/of/2006/1024/pdf/OFR-2006-1024.pdf)

## CO2 enhanced oil recovery (miscible CO2 flooding)
<a id="ups-co2-eor"></a>

CO2 is compressed and injected into an oil reservoir, usually one already waterflooded, where at or above the minimum miscibility pressure it mixes with the crude, swells it and lowers its viscosity so that residual oil can be swept to producing wells. Water and CO2 are often injected in alternating slugs (WAG). Produced CO2 is separated from oil and water, recompressed and reinjected, so most purchased CO2 stays underground.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SRC["CO2 supply<br/><small>natural CO2 source or captured industrial CO2, pipeline</small>"]
    CMP["Compression and dehydration<br/><small>CO2 compressors, dehydration</small>"]
    INJ["Injection wells (CO2 and water, alternating in WAG)<br/><small>injection wells, wellhead manifolds</small>"]
    RES["Reservoir: miscible displacement"]
    PRD["Production wells and test manifold<br/><small>producing wells, well production manifold</small>"]
    SEP["Separation of oil, water and gas<br/><small>separators, gas treating</small>"]
    RCY["Recycle compression<br/><small>recycle compressor</small>"]
    OIL["Oil to sales"]
    SRC --> CMP
    CMP --> INJ
    INJ --> RES
    RES --> PRD
    PRD --> SEP
    SEP -->|incremental oil| OIL
    SEP -->|produced CO2| RCY
    RCY --> INJ
```

**Key equipment:** CO2 compressors, CO2 pipeline, injection and production wells, production manifold, oil-water-gas separators, recycle compressor.

**Performance:**
- incremental oil recovery over primary and secondary recovery: 4-15 % of original oil in place; NETL primer; primary plus secondary typically 30-35% OOIP; one pilot reported up to 22%. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- water to CO2 injection ratio in WAG: 0.5-4 volumes of water per volume of CO2 at reservoir conditions; NETL primer; slug size 0.1-2% of pore volume. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- cumulative CO2 injected: 15-30 % of hydrocarbon pore volume; NETL primer. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- share of oil cost per barrel taken by CO2 purchase and recycle: 25-50 % of cost per barrel produced; NETL primer. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- reservoir depth screening range: 2000-9800 ft; NETL primer screening table (other criteria: oil gravity above 27-30 °API, viscosity at or below 10-12 cp, pressure above 1,200-1,500 psia, residual oil saturation above 0.25-0.30). [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- CO2 injected per barrel of oil produced (United States fields): 0.3-0.6 t CO2/bbl; varies between fields and across the life of projects; dollar year not stated. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/181b48b4-323f-454d-96fb-0bb1889d96a9/CCUS_in_clean_energy_transitions.pdf)

**Costs:**
- illustrative pre-tax economic margin: 15-25 USD/bbl (2008$); NETL primer example: oil USD 70/bbl, net wellhead USD 55, capital amortization USD 5-10, CO2 USD 15 (USD 2/Mcf purchase, USD 0.70/Mcf recycle), operations USD 10-15; dollar year not stated, data to 2008. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- CO2 purchase cost per barrel of oil produced (0.5 t CO2/bbl injected): 7.5-15 USD/bbl (2020$); purchased CO2 for EOR priced around USD 15-30/t CO2, generally indexed to the oil price; injecting 0.5 t CO2 per barrel of oil produced; dollar year not stated. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/181b48b4-323f-454d-96fb-0bb1889d96a9/CCUS_in_clean_energy_transitions.pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- Weyburn field, Saskatchewan (EnCana), CA: about 95 million ft3/d CO2 (4,935 t/d) from the Dakota Gasification plant via a 205-mile pipeline; 130 million bbl incremental oil expected; operating as of the primer (data to 2008). [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- Wasson field Denver Unit, West Texas (Shell), US: 31,500 bbl/d of which 26,850 bbl/d incremental from CO2; over 120 million bbl incremental to 2008; operating as of 2008. [source](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf)
- Sukowati field, Bojonegoro (Pertamina EP, with JOGMEC and JAPEX), ID: pilot: about 500 t CO2 huff-and-puff into well SKW-18 over 7 days; first CO2 injection 7 December 2023 (500 t huff-and-puff pilot into well SKW-18); inter-well CO2 injection field trial started 8 October 2024 under an August 2024 joint study by Pertamina EP, JAPEX and JOGMEC; full-scale application is still being evaluated by the joint study and Pertamina EP Cepu (operator of the adjacent Jambaran Tiung Biru field) as of October 2026, with commercial CO2-EOR not yet running. [source](https://www.chemanalyst.com/NewsAndDeals/NewsDetails/japex-and-partners-launch-co2-injection-test-at-sukowati-oil-field-in-indonesia-30847)

TRL source: [IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide, dataset of 24 July 2026, 2025 assessment (IEA 1-11 scale): 'CO2-enhanced oil recovery' 9](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide)

Sources: [1](https://www.netl.doe.gov/sites/default/files/netl-file/co2_eor_primer.pdf), [2](https://www.migas.esdm.go.id/post/implementasikan-ccus-lapangan-sukowati-injeksi-co2-perdana), [3](https://www.chemanalyst.com/NewsAndDeals/NewsDetails/japex-and-partners-launch-co2-injection-test-at-sukowati-oil-field-in-indonesia-30847), [4](https://suaradesa.co/energi/ccus-sukowati-dorong-optimalisasi-produksi-migas-melalui-inovasi-teknologi-dan-pengelolaan-karbon-yang-bertanggung-jawab/), [5](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide). Last reviewed 2026-10-28.

## Casing drilling and expandable tubulars
<a id="ups-casing-drilling-expandable-tubulars"></a>

Expandable tubulars are run and then enlarged by pulling or pushing an expansion tool through them, cutting the diameter lost with each casing string and aiming at a monobore well. The Wikipedia page read does not cover casing drilling (drilling with the casing itself).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    RUN["Run the expandable liner<br/><small>liner hanger</small>"]
    EXP["Expand hydraulically or mechanically<br/><small>expansion cone</small>"]
    SET["Seal and cement<br/><small>cement</small>"]
    RUN --> EXP
    EXP --> SET
```

**Key equipment:** expandable liner or clad, expansion tool, liner hanger.

**Performance:**
- expansion ratio: 20-30 %; the page gives both 20% and 30%. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expandable_tubular_technology)
- expansion length: 30-6000 ft; cold-working expansion. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expandable_tubular_technology)

**Reference plants:**
- Occidental Petroleum of Qatar — directional casing while drilling at Idd Shargi North Dome (offshore Qatar), QA: 12-1/4-in. horizontal production and injection wells landed up to 86 deg inclination into the top of the Shuaiba, with 9-5/8-in. casing drilled and set through the unstable Nahr Umr shale; operating. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/drilling-completion/article/16757958/directional-casing-while-drilling-eases-path-through-nahr-umr-shale-off-qatar)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (offshore-mag casing drilling articles: 'Casing drilling marks a century of progress' and 'Directional casing while drilling off Qatar'); only qualitative cost-reduction claims, no dollar figures stated. ## Chemical EOR (polymer and surfactant flooding)
<a id="ups-chemical-eor"></a>

Chemicals are added to injection water to recover oil that waterflooding leaves behind. Polymers such as hydrolyzed polyacrylamide (HPAM) raise the viscosity of the injected water so it sweeps the reservoir more evenly and water breaks through later. Surfactants lower the interfacial tension between oil and water so trapped oil droplets can move. Alkali-surfactant-polymer (ASP) combines both. Polymer and surfactant performance depends on salinity, temperature, adsorption on the rock and injectivity, so candidates are screened in the laboratory and by core flooding before a field pilot.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MK["Chemical make-up<br/><small>polymer or surfactant dissolution and dilution skids, mixing tanks</small>"]
    FLT["Filtration and quality control<br/><small>filters, viscosity monitoring</small>"]
    INJ["Injection<br/><small>injection pumps, injection wells</small>"]
    RES["Reservoir: mobility control and oil mobilisation"]
    PRD["Production and separation<br/><small>producing wells, oil-water separators, produced water treatment</small>"]
    OIL["Oil to sales"]
    WTR["Produced water back to make-up"]
    MK --> FLT
    FLT --> INJ
    INJ --> RES
    RES --> PRD
    PRD -->|incremental oil| OIL
    PRD -->|produced water for reinjection| WTR
    WTR --> MK
```

**Key equipment:** polymer or surfactant make-up units, filters, injection pumps, injection and production wells, produced water treatment.

**Performance:**
- incremental recovery, HPAM Polymer C at 3000 ppm, laboratory core floods: 8.8-10 % (two cores); Five commercial HPAM polymers tested at 500-5000 ppm, salinity 3,000-200,000 ppm, up to 90 °C; laboratory only. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2125)
- average incremental recovery of HPAM injection in Daqing field blocks (field): 11 % over waterflooding; as reported in the HPAM article's introduction, which calls Daqing the world's largest commercial polymer flood; original study not read. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2125)
- incremental recovery factor from palm-oil MES surfactant with 96% ethanol, laboratory coreflood: 29.24 % over waterflooding; total recovery factor 45.90%; MES 0.5-2.0%, best formulation 1.25% MES; laboratory only. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2096)
- interfacial tension achieved: 0.521 mN/m; MES with ethanol co-surfactant. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2096)
- incremental recovery after 0.35 PV HPAM polymer slug following waterflood, light-oil coreflood: 13-13.5 % OOIP incremental over waterflood; HPAM 500-1,500 ppm (1,000 ppm recommended) in brine of roughly 2,800 ppm salinity at 70 C; waterflood alone 38.7% OOIP; about 22% of residual oil in place; 500 ppm avoided plugging (FR 1.1, RRF near 1); laboratory coreflood only. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/1181/964)
- oil recovery factor after 3-year waterflood plus 17-year polymer flood, Air Benakat formation model, South Sumatra (simulation): 45.83-80.46 % OOIP; CMG-STARS, 1,500 ppm polymer after 3 years of waterflooding; 59.86% homogeneous model, 45.83/69.27/80.46% for Lorenz coefficient 0.2/0.4/0.6; simulation only. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/1334/pdf)
- incremental recovery from microemulsion ASP plus polymer drive after waterflood, Bentheimer coreflood: 57.71 % of initial oil saturation (Soi); 2% surfactant A + 0.85% Na2CO3 (ASP) with 2,200 ppm polymer; 0.15 PV ASP slug then 1.5 PV polymer drive at 85 C; waterflood alone 38.21% Soi, total 95.93% Soi, i.e. 93.41% of remaining oil; salinity conditioned by synthetic softened brine preflush; laboratory only. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/43/pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- Daqing oilfield polymer flood, CN: average incremental recovery about 11% over waterflooding across tested blocks; described as the world's largest commercial polymer flood. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2125)
- PETRONAS Carigali and Shell Malaysia EOR program, Baram Delta (Sarawak) and North Sabah PSCs, offshore Malaysia, MY: HOA aims to lift production by 90,000-100,000 boe/d across the Baram Delta and North Sabah fields, raising average recovery factor from 36% to 50% and extending field life beyond 2040; technology deployed at North Sabah flagged as possibly the world's first field-scale offshore chemical EOR process; planned. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/production/article/16775227/petronas-shell-consider-first-ever-field-scale-chemical-eor-offshore-malaysia)

**Missing inputs:**
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA guide has no chemical EOR entry; 'commercial' rests on the Daqing statement in the HPAM article, which was read only second-hand. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched chemical EOR, polymer flood, surfactant, alkaline, enhanced oil recovery); 2026-10-28 Web-checked 2026-10-28 (DOAJ search: chemical enhanced oil recovery polymer); independent open review found - Podoprigora, Byazrov and Sytnik, Energies 15(21):8300, 2022, covering pilot and full-scale SP/ASP field tests in China, Canada, Russia, Hungary and Oman (abstract via DOAJ; MDPI full text CDN-blocked) - but no TRL number stated and the original Daqing study was not open. Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open); Peer-reviewed reviews of chemical EOR field projects (SPE, open journals) (catalog: doaj, open). Next: Find an independent review that lists field projects, and read the original Daqing study.
- chemical cost per barrel of incremental oil and project capex, with dollar year and basis (costs): No source read gives costs. Searched LEMIGAS SCOG corpus for cost, USD, capex, price, economics across the four polymer/surfactant papers; they state no dollar cost per barrel of incremental oil or project capex, only qualitative remarks such as 1,500 ppm striking 'a fair compromise between ... lowering the cost of the polymer injection'; 2026-10-28 Web-checked 2026-10-28 (DOAJ searches: polymer flooding economic analysis field; Frontiers in Energy Research 2023 Bohai B oilfield discontinuous chemical flooding paper read); sources describe cost savings qualitatively, no open source states dollar cost per barrel of incremental oil or project capex. Where: Open techno-economic papers (arXiv, LEMIGAS SCOG archive, Universitas Trisakti publications) (catalog: lemigas-scog, open). Next: Add cost per incremental barrel with dollar year.

Sources: [1](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2125), [2](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2096), [3](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/1181/964), [4](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/1334/pdf), [5](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/download/43/pdf), [6](https://www.offshore-mag.com/production/article/16775227/petronas-shell-consider-first-ever-field-scale-chemical-eor-offshore-malaysia). ## Coal bed methane
<a id="ups-coal-bed-methane"></a>

Wells in coal seams 100 to 1,500 m deep are dewatered to lower pressure so methane desorbs, giving a rising gas profile after start-up. US coal bed methane was 1.76 Tcf in 2017 (3.6% of dry gas), down from a 1.97 Tcf peak in 2008; Queensland supplied about 10% of Australia's gas by 2014.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRILL["Drill and complete in coal seam<br/><small>well</small>"]
    DEW["Pump water to depressurise<br/><small>pump</small>"]
    GAS["Gas flows to compression<br/><small>compressor station</small>"]
    DRILL --> DEW
    DEW --> GAS
```

**Key equipment:** coal seam wells, dewatering pumps, water handling, gas compression.

**Performance:**
- US CBM production, 2017: 1.76 Tcf; 3.6% of dry gas; peak 1.97 Tcf in 2008. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalbed_methane)
- well peak output: 8500 m3/day (about); 300,000 cf/d. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalbed_methane)
- peak gas rate per coalbed methane well: 236-319 Mcf/d; About 6,600 Powder River Basin CBM wells: average peak 319 Mcf/d (median 236), reached on average 1.2 years after first production.. [source](https://onepetro.org/SPERMPTC/proceedings/07RMTC/All-07RMTC/SPE-107308-MS/142057)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (coal bed methane techno-economic cost study; search engines returned bot-block pages, Queensland and Geoscience Australia pages unreachable or 404); no open source gives CBM capex/opex. ## Coiled tubing and well intervention
<a id="ups-coiled-tubing-intervention"></a>

A continuous steel pipe (normally 1 to 3.25 inch) on a reel is pushed into live wells for cleanouts, chemical placement, milling, logging and drilling. It trips faster than jointed pipe and acts as a nearly closed system, but cannot trip jointed pipe and needs a larger offshore footprint than wireline.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    RIG["Rig up coiled-tubing unit and well control stack<br/><small>injector head, reel, BOP stack</small>"]
    RUN["Run the coil and circulate or mill<br/><small>bottom-hole assembly, pumps</small>"]
    POOH["Pull and spool back"]
    RIG --> RUN
    RUN --> POOH
```

**Key equipment:** coiled-tubing reel, injector head, BOP stack, bottom-hole assembly.

**Performance:**
- coiled-tubing diameter: 1-3.25 in; 25 to 83 mm. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coiled_tubing)
- steel yield strength: 55000-120000 psi; coil grades. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coiled_tubing)

**Reference plants:**
- Statoil (now Equinor) first North Sea HP/HT coiled tubing milling job, Kvitebjoern field, block 34/11, Norwegian continental shelf, NO: well 34/11-A-9 T2, gas/condensate producer with reservoir at about 4,000 m, 770 bar and 160 C; CT milling of a stuck-closed isolation ball valve; operating. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/business-briefs/equipment-engineering/article/16761449/statoil-records-first-successful-north-sea-hp-ht-coiled-tubing-milling-job)

**Missing inputs:**
- day rate or job cost (costs): Not stated. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (coiled tubing day rate and job cost; DOAJ search 'coiled tubing intervention cost' returned only fatigue-modeling papers, no figures); no open source states a day rate or job cost - intervention day-rate surveys are paid. Where: Operator intervention cost studies (open). Next: Add typical cost with year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched coiled tubing, well intervention, workover, downhole); 2026-10-28 Web-checked 2026-10-28 (coiled tubing technology readiness level TRL; DuckDuckGo and Brave returned bot-block pages); no open TRL table found. ## Digital core and special core analysis
<a id="ups-digital-core-analysis"></a>

Cores are imaged by whole-core CT (0.5 mm slices), micro-CT and FIB-SEM, and the 3D images are used to compute porosity, permeability, capillary pressure and relative permeability in simulations. Schlumberger reports that analysis taking weeks or months can finish in hours or days.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IMG["Image core at several scales<br/><small>whole-core CT, micro-CT, FIB-SEM</small>"]
    SIM["Simulate pore-scale flow<br/><small>multiphase pore-flow simulator</small>"]
    VAL["Validate against laboratory tests<br/><small>SCAL laboratory</small>"]
    IMG --> SIM
    SIM --> VAL
```

**Key equipment:** CT and micro-CT scanners, FIB-SEM, HPC simulation, SCAL laboratory.

**Performance:**
- whole-core CT slice resolution: 0.5 mm; 4-inch core in liner. [source](https://geoexpro.com/inside-the-rock/)

**Reference plants:**
- Sonatrach commercial use of Ingrain digital rock physics services, DZ: commercial agreement covering Ingrain's digital rock physics services (3D imaging and computation of reservoir rock properties); operating. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/exploration-geosciences/article/16789934/sonatrach-to-use-ingrains-digital-rock-physics-technology)

**Missing inputs:**
- service price (costs): Not published. Where: Vendor quotations (paid). Next: Leave unless a published price is found.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched digital rock, core analysis, digital core, digital twin); 2026-10-28 Web-checked 2026-10-28 (digital rock physics technology readiness level TRL; DuckDuckGo and Brave returned bot-block pages); no open TRL table found. Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://geoexpro.com/inside-the-rock/), [2](https://www.offshore-mag.com/exploration-geosciences/article/16789934/sonatrach-to-use-ingrains-digital-rock-physics-technology), [3](https://www.halliburton.com/en/resources/fastscal-digital-scal-improves-resource-estimates). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Electric instead of gas-pneumatic controllers
<a id="ups-electric-pneumatic-controllers"></a>

Gas-driven pneumatic controllers vent methane to operate valves; replacing them with instrument air or electric controllers removes that venting. EPA Natural Gas STAR reports gas pneumatic devices vented about 51 Bcf/yr in production (2006) and a conversion typically costs $45,000 to $75,000 with payback in months at $7/Mcf.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    AUDIT["Inventory gas-driven controllers<br/><small>emissions survey</small>"]
    CONV["Install instrument air or electric controllers<br/><small>air compressor, electric actuators</small>"]
    AUDIT --> CONV
```

**Key equipment:** instrument air compressor and dryer, electric controllers, solar and battery power at remote sites.

**Performance:**
- gas vented by pneumatic controllers in US production: 51 Bcf/yr; 2006; transmission about 14 Bcf/yr. [source](https://19january2021snapshot.epa.gov/sites/static/files/2016-06/documents/ll_instrument_air.pdf)
- methane emission rate per pneumatic controller: 0.68-19.25 scf/h; Measured over 72 devices: high-bleed 19.25 scf/h (n=8), low-bleed 0.68 scf/h (n=19), all controllers 4.98 scf/h. Electric controllers vent nothing by design.. [source](https://pubs.acs.org/estlcu/article/6/6/348/507411/Multiday-Measurements-of-Pneumatic-Controller)

**Costs:**
- typical instrument-air conversion cost: 45000-75000 USD (2006$); 2006 dollars. [source](https://19january2021snapshot.epa.gov/sites/static/files/2016-06/documents/ll_instrument_air.pdf)

TRL source: [IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide dataset (July 2026), entry 'Instrumented air systems'](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide)

**Missing inputs:**
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no operator press release names a specific controller-conversion project with counts. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.

## Electric submersible pumps
<a id="ups-electric-submersible-pumps"></a>

A downhole multistage centrifugal pump driven by a submersible motor on a cable lifts 16 to 4,770 m³/d (100 to 30,000 bbl/d). SLB reports an installed base of 150,000 to 200,000 wells and notes ESPs are usually uneconomic below about 25 m³/d.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PWR["Surface drive sends power down the cable<br/><small>VSD, transformer, power cable</small>"]
    MOT["Motor turns pump stages<br/><small>submersible motor, seal section</small>"]
    LIFT["Pump lifts fluid; gas separator removes free gas<br/><small>pump, gas separator</small>"]
    PWR --> MOT
    MOT --> LIFT
```

**Key equipment:** ESP pump, motor, seal section, gas separator, power cable, variable speed drive.

**Performance:**
- flow rate range: 16-4770 m3/d; 100 to 30,000 bbl/d. [source](https://www.slb.com/-/media/files/oilfield-review/defining-esp.ashx)
- installed ESP wells worldwide: 150000-200000 wells (estimate); SLB Oilfield Review. [source](https://www.slb.com/-/media/files/oilfield-review/defining-esp.ashx)
- ESP depth capability: 3000 m (about 10,000 ft); Wikipedia comparison table. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- average ESP run life: 1058 days; Average ESP lifespan of 1,058 days in the offshore field studied, with half of all ESPs below 1,000 days.. [source](https://onepetro.org/SJ/article/31/03/1802/794801/Lifespan-Prediction-and-Multiobjective)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no OEM/operator release names a specific ESP installation with counts. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). ## Extended-reach and multilateral wells
<a id="ups-extended-reach-multilateral-wells"></a>

Extended-reach drilling (ERD) reaches a large reservoir area from one surface location by drilling very long deviated wells; multilateral wells add branches from a main bore. The record ERD well (Orlan platform, Sakhalin-1, 24 April 2015) has a measured depth of 13,500 m and a horizontal displacement of 12,030 m.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PLAN["Plan trajectory and torque and drag<br/><small>well planning software</small>"]
    DRILL["Drill with RSS and managed hydraulics<br/><small>rotary steerable system, high-capacity rig</small>"]
    COMP["Complete, with junctions in multilaterals<br/><small>liner, junction</small>"]
    PLAN --> DRILL
    DRILL --> COMP
```

**Key equipment:** high-capacity rig, rotary steerable system, torque-and-drag management, junction systems for multilaterals.

**Performance:**
- record ERD measured depth: 13500 m; Orlan, Sakhalin-1, April 2015. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_reach_drilling)
- record ERD horizontal displacement: 12030-12130 m; Orlan 12,030 m (2015); earlier Yastreb well 12,130 m (2014). [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_reach_drilling)

**Reference plants:**
- Odoptu OP-11 extended-reach well (Sakhalin-1, Exxon Neftegas), RU: world-record extended-reach well at completion: 12,345 m measured depth (40,502 ft); operating. [source](https://offshore-energy.biz/2011/01/28/exxon-drills-the-worlds-longest-extended-reach-well-offshore-sakhalin-russia)

**Missing inputs:**
- cost versus several conventional wells (costs): Not stated. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (SPE ERD papers OnePetro-restricted); no open published cost comparison found. Where: SPE ERD papers (catalog: onepetro, open). Next: Add a published cost comparison.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched multilateral, extended reach, horizontal well, drilling); 2026-10-28 Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_reach_drilling), [2](https://jpt.spe.org/inflow-control-device-inflow-control-valves-aid-kuwaits-first-smart-multilateral-well), [3](https://offshore-energy.biz/2011/01/28/exxon-drills-the-worlds-longest-extended-reach-well-offshore-sakhalin-russia), [4](https://petroedgeasia.net/blog/extended-reach-drilling-erd-complete-guide-to-planning-execution-and-risk-control). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## FPSO and floating production (TLP, spar, semi-submersible)
<a id="ups-fpso-floating-production"></a>

A floating production, storage and offloading vessel processes, stores and offloads oil, usually moored by a turret; TLPs, spars and semi-submersibles host dry or wet trees in deeper water. Over 270 FPSOs were in service (a 2019 figure), and a new build can cost up to US$1 billion.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    RISER["Receive fluids via risers<br/><small>turret, risers</small>"]
    PROC["Process oil, gas and water<br/><small>separators, gas compression</small>"]
    STORE["Store and offload to shuttle tanker<br/><small>cargo tanks, offloading hose</small>"]
    RISER --> PROC
    PROC --> STORE
```

**Key equipment:** hull, turret and mooring, topsides process, storage tanks, offloading system, ship-shaped FPSO, semi-submersible production platform, SPAR production platform, TLP production topsides, FLNG, turret mooring.

**Performance:**
- oil FPSOs in service: 270 units (over); 2019 figure cited by Wikipedia. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floating_production_storage_and_offloading)
- Kizomba A storage: 2.2 million barrels; built by Hyundai Heavy Industries for over US$800 million. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floating_production_storage_and_offloading)
- TLP depth limit: 2000 m (about); Perdido spar is in 2,438 m. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform)

**Costs:**
- new-build FPSO: 1000 USD million (up to); dollar year not stated; conversions can cost under US$100 million. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floating_production_storage_and_offloading)
- announced capital investment: 12 AUD (USD 8.71 billion equivalent as stat; Investment announced by Shell to build the Prelude FLNG facility; source gives announcement date 20 May 2011 but no explicit price-year label  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Floating_liquefied_natural_gas&action=raw)
- analyst estimated vessel cost: 10.8-12.6 USD; Industry analyst estimate of Prelude vessel cost reported by Reuters; article dated 4 December 2013  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://www.bbc.com/news/technology-25213845)
- initial project cost: 34 USD billion; Initial cost of the integrated LNG project (offshore CPF + FPSO + 889 km pipeline + onshore liquefaction plant)  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ichthys_gas_field&action=raw)
- Total project CAPEX / investment at FID: 3.4e+10 USD billion (as printed) (2012$); INPEX and Total FID announcement: 'US$34 billion Ichthys LNG Project'; CAPEX line in INPEX project summary Source dated 13 January 2012 and states the figure at FID; source does not separately label a nominal-dollar base year.. [source](https://www.inpex.com.au/news-and-updates/media-centre/media-releases/final-investment-decision-on-ichthys-lng-project/)
- Total project investment at FID (JV partner statement): 3.4e+10 USD billion (as printed) (2012$); Total/TotalEnergies press release: 'investment of US$34 billion dollars'; Total holds 24% Press release dated 13 January 2012; base dollar year not separately labelled.. [source](https://totalenergies.com/newsroom/australie-total-et-inpex-lancent-ichthys-projet-strategique-de-gnl-offshore/?lang=eng)
- FLNG vessel EPC contract value (Samsung regulatory filing): 3.27e+12 KRW; 3.27 trillion won contract for Technip Samsung Consortium to engineer, procure, construct and install the vessel; article prints '($3 billion)' conversion Contract period 1 June 2011 to 30 September 2016, as stated in the filing; base year not separately labelled.. [source](https://gcaptain.com/samsung-shells-flng-facility/)
- Cost (project tracker): 1.6e+09 USD; LNG2030 project tracker record for PFLNG Dua: 'Cost (US$bn) 1.6' Dollar price year not stated by the source; tracker entry last reviewed 24 July 2026. Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://lng2030.com/project/lng_223)

**Reference plants:**
- FPSO Kikeh (Kikeh field, offshore Sabah), MY: 120,000 bopd process capacity; water depth about 1,300 m; first deepwater FPSO in Malaysia and Asia; operating. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/493811-pttep-awards-long-term-charter-extension-for-fpso-kikeh)

**Missing inputs:**
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched FPSO, floating production, floating storage, turret); 2026-10-28 Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Fixed jackets and gravity-based structures
<a id="ups-fixed-jackets-gravity-structures"></a>

Fixed platforms stand on steel jackets piled to the seabed or on concrete gravity-based structures (GBS) that can store oil; they are economic to about 520 m of water. Bullwinkle (Gulf of Mexico) is the tallest fixed example at 529 m, and Hibernia's GBS weighs about 1.2 million tons with 1.3 million barrels of storage.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    FAB["Fabricate jacket or GBS<br/><small>fabrication yard</small>"]
    INST["Float out, install and pile or ballast<br/><small>heavy-lift vessel, piling hammers</small>"]
    TOP["Install topsides<br/><small>crane vessel</small>"]
    FAB --> INST
    INST --> TOP
```

**Key equipment:** steel jacket or concrete GBS, piles, topsides, risers.

**Performance:**
- fixed-platform economic depth limit: 520 m; about 1,710 ft. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform)
- Hibernia GBS weight: 1.2 million tons; 111 m tall; 1.3 million bbl storage. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform)
- Troll A GBS height: 472 m; 300 m water depth, built 1996. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform)

**Reference plants:**
- Troll A Condeep gravity-based structure (Troll field, Norwegian North Sea), NO: natural-gas production platform; gravity-based substructure about 472 m total height in about 300 m water depth, about 245,000 m3 concrete, about 683,600 t unballasted weight; operating. [source](https://www.akersolutions.com/what-we-do/fixed-and-floating-solutions/gbs/gbs-cases/troll-a)

**Missing inputs:**
- jacket and topsides cost per tonne with year (costs): Not on the page. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open source states jacket/topside cost per tonne (IHS data paid). Where: Offshore Magazine and IHS cost data (open). Next: Add a cost reference.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched jacket, gravity structure, fixed platform, topside, offshore structure); 2026-10-28 Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform), [2](https://www.akersolutions.com/what-we-do/fixed-and-floating-solutions/gbs/gbs-cases/troll-a), [3](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Flare gas recovery and zero routine flaring
<a id="ups-flare-gas-recovery"></a>

Gas that would be burned in a flare is instead collected, compressed and treated so that it can be sold, used for power or reinjected. Vapour recovery units capture low-pressure methane streams from tanks and compressor seals. Where no pipeline exists, small-scale GTL or power generation can use the gas on site. Routine, non-emergency flaring can then be ended.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    COL["Gas collection<br/><small>flare header, liquid seal</small>"]
    CMP["Compression<br/><small>flare gas recovery compressors, vapour recovery units</small>"]
    TRT["Treating<br/><small>knockout drum, cooling and gas conditioning</small>"]
    USE["Sale, power or reinjection"]
    FLR["Emergency flare"]
    COL --> CMP
    CMP --> TRT
    TRT -->|recovered gas| USE
    TRT -->|surplus to flare| FLR
```

**Key equipment:** flare gas recovery compressors, liquid seal drum, vapour recovery units, gas treating skid, flare tip.

**Performance:**
- natural gas flared globally each year: 150 bcm; majority is routine, non-emergency flaring; IEA 2025 tracker. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- hydrocarbon vapour capture by vapour recovery units: 95 % of tank hydrocarbon vapours (up to); EPA Lessons Learned case study: vapor recovery can capture up to 95% of hydrocarbon vapors from tanks; recovered vapours usable as fuel or sale gas. Specific flare-gas-recovery-unit turndown figures were not found in open supplier datasheets. [source](https://19january2021snapshot.epa.gov/sites/static/files/2017-08/documents/richards_midland_2006.pdf)
- flare gas recovery unit product recovery: 94 %; ASPEN HYSYS simulation of a 430,600 kg/h refinery FGRU: 94% average product recovery; sliding-vane recovery compressors give about 3:1 turndown.. [source](https://biomedres.us/fulltexts/BJSTR.MS.ID.007881.php)

TRL source: [IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide, dataset of 24 July 2026, 2025 assessment (IEA 1-11 scale): 'Flare gas recovery' 9](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide)

**Missing inputs:**
- capex per unit and payback with dollar year (costs): The tracker gives the flared volume only; the collected IEA documents state no capex per unit or payback for flare gas recovery and the World Bank GFMR reports were not collected. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (World Bank Zero Routine Flaring materials argue economic viability but state no per-unit capex or payback). Where: IEA Global Methane Tracker documentation (collected) (catalog: iea-methane-tracker, open); World Bank Global Gas Flaring Reduction partnership reports (open). Next: Add capex and payback with basis.
- named flare reduction projects in Malaysia and Indonesia (reference_plants): None read. Web-checked 2026-10-28; PETRONAS/Pertamina sustainability pages state ZRF commitments without named per-site recovery projects. Where: PETRONAS and Pertamina sustainability reports (catalog: petronas-tech, open). Next: Collect the reports and add a project with year and volume.

Sources: [1](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf), [2](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Full-waveform inversion and seismic imaging
<a id="ups-full-waveform-inversion"></a>

An initial velocity model is updated iteratively until simulated seismic waveforms match recorded ones, producing a high-resolution subsurface velocity model that feeds migration and imaging. Compute needs are large and the method can fail when the starting model is too far off (cycle skipping); the SEG page read explains the geotechnical form of the method only.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MOD["Start from an initial velocity model<br/><small>velocity model</small>"]
    SIM["Simulate waves and compare with recorded data<br/><small>wave-equation solver, HPC cluster</small>"]
    UPD["Update the model by minimising misfit and repeat<br/><small>optimiser</small>"]
    MOD --> SIM
    SIM --> UPD
```

**Key equipment:** HPC cluster or GPUs, wave-equation solver, velocity model building software.

**Performance:**
- FWI frequency band and compute cost: 4-28 Hz in 15.9 hours on one GPU; 4-28 Hz data inverted in three bands over 34 iterations, completed in 15.9 hours on a single laptop GPU.. [source](https://bfienergygroup.ca/full-waveform-inversion)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; survey acquisition costs are commercial; no open unit cost found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no named survey with figures on open pages. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). ## Gas injection and WAG (hydrocarbon, CO2, nitrogen)
<a id="ups-gas-injection-wag"></a>

Gas (natural gas, nitrogen or CO2) is injected to maintain pressure or become miscible with oil; water-alternating-gas (WAG) cycles gas and water to improve sweep. Gas injection projects made up 56% of the 374 EOR projects counted worldwide in 2017 (CO2 44%, other gas 12%).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    GAS["Supply and compress gas<br/><small>compressors</small>"]
    INJ["Inject gas or alternate gas and water<br/><small>injection wells, WAG manifolds</small>"]
    PROD["Produce and separate; recycle gas<br/><small>separators, recompression</small>"]
    GAS --> INJ
    INJ --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** gas compressors, injection wells, WAG manifolds, separation and recycle.

**Performance:**
- share of 2017 EOR projects using CO2: 44 % of 374 projects; other gas injection 12%. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_oil_recovery)
- CO2 EOR effective depth: 2000 ft (deeper than); depth threshold for miscible CO2 flooding as stated in the source. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_oil_recovery)
- WAG incremental recovery factor: 5-20 % of OOIP; WAG pilots typically yield 5-10% of OOIP incremental recovery, with up to 20% in a few pilots.. [source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s13202-019-0694-x)

**Reference plants:**
- Statfjord WAG (water-alternating-gas injection), NO: largest oil field in the North Sea at start-up (production began 23 Nov 1979); injection converted from water-only to WAG in the late 1990s; operating. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/archive/2003/03/17/BoostingRecoveryWithFoam)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). ## Gas lift
<a id="ups-gas-lift"></a>

Compressed gas is injected into the production tubing through valves in mandrels to lighten the fluid column so the well flows. About 10% of US oil wells with insufficient reservoir pressure use gas lift, and side-pocket mandrels let valves be changed by wireline.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    COMP["Compress lift gas<br/><small>compressor</small>"]
    INJ["Inject through gas-lift valves<br/><small>side-pocket mandrels, valves</small>"]
    PROD["Produce and separate; recycle the gas<br/><small>separator</small>"]
    COMP --> INJ
    INJ --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** gas compressor, gas-lift mandrels and valves, injection manifold.

**Performance:**
- US oil wells using gas lift: 10 % (about); wells with insufficient reservoir pressure. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_lift)
- gas lift depth and rate capability: 4600 m (up to 15,000 ft); up to 30,000 bbl/d; Wikipedia comparison. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- overall system efficiency of gas lift vs other artificial lift methods: 35 % overall system efficiency; IPIECA Energy Efficiency Compendium, artificial lift entry (2023), Table 1: gas lift 35% overall system efficiency; comparison: sucker rod pumps 45-60%, progressive cavity pumps 70-80%, ESPs 30-70%, hydraulic pumps 20-35%, plunger lift 75-85%. The compendium defines injected-gas scf/bbl-of-liquid as the KPI but publishes no benchmark value for it. [source](https://www.ipieca.org/resources/energy-efficiency-compendium/artificial-lift-2023)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). ## Hydraulic fracturing (plug-and-perf, electric frac fleets)
<a id="ups-hydraulic-fracturing"></a>

High-pressure fluid and proppant open fractures along a cased and perforated lateral in stages; plug-and-perf isolates each stage with a plug, while sliding sleeves open one at a time. Typical fluid is about 90% water, 9.5% sand and 0.5% additives, and pumps reach 100 MPa (15,000 psi).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PERF["Perforate a stage<br/><small>wireline perforating guns</small>"]
    PUMP["Pump fluid and proppant<br/><small>frac pumps, blender</small>"]
    PLUG["Set plug to isolate stage and repeat<br/><small>composite plug</small>"]
    PERF --> PUMP
    PUMP --> PLUG
```

**Key equipment:** frac pumps (diesel, dual fuel or electric), blender, wireline unit, composite plugs, sand and water supply.

**Performance:**
- water per well: 1.2-3.5 million US gallons; up to 5 million for large projects. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydraulic_fracturing)
- maximum pump pressure: 100 MPa (15,000 psi); and 265 L/s. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydraulic_fracturing)
- emissions of electric fleet vs Tier 2 diesel: 30-45 % lower; 30% on reciprocating-engine power, up to 45% on ERCOT grid; Halliburton estimate. [source](https://www.halliburton.com/en/completions/stimulation/hydraulic-fracturing/zeus-fracturing-services)
- ZEUS pump unit hydraulic horsepower: 5000 HHP (up to); vendor. [source](https://www.halliburton.com/en/completions/stimulation/hydraulic-fracturing/zeus-fracturing-services)

**Missing inputs:**
- completion cost per stage or per well with year (costs): Neither page gives it; the collected EIA document (AEO2026 Electricity Market Module assumptions, April 2026) contains no well-cost, completion-cost or drilling data (it covers power-plant technology costs only). Web-checked 2026-10-28: per-stage pricing ranges (about USD 30,000-70,000/stage; electric frac fleet USD 40-60 m) appear only in finance/trade guides without a primary open source; not recorded. Where: EIA drilling productivity and well cost studies (catalog: eia-reports, open). Next: Add a well-cost figure with year.

Web-checked 2026-10-28; no named completion campaign with per-well figures on open pages. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). ## Intelligent completions and inflow control devices
<a id="ups-intelligent-completions"></a>

Downhole valves, gauges and inflow control devices let operators monitor and adjust production from each zone without intervention. In Kuwait's first smart multilateral well, two 10-position hydraulic valves and nozzle-type ICDs controlled two laterals to fight early water breakthrough.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MON["Gauges report pressure and temperature<br/><small>permanent downhole gauges</small>"]
    CTRL["Valves choke or open each zone<br/><small>inflow control valves (ICV), ICDs</small>"]
    OPT["Optimise water-free production"]
    MON --> CTRL
    CTRL --> OPT
```

**Key equipment:** inflow control valves, inflow control devices, downhole gauges, control lines.

**Performance:**
- ICV positions: 10 positions per valve; two ICVs, one per lateral, Kuwait Minagish. [source](https://jpt.spe.org/inflow-control-device-inflow-control-valves-aid-kuwaits-first-smart-multilateral-well)
- CP+ICD water-shutoff trial result, Liuhua 4-1 Well A4H4, offshore China: 14-78 % water production index of baseline (14); % WOR reduction vs pre-trial (78); well had been shut in Dec 2015 after producing 99.5% BS&W; after the continuous pack-off ICD trial filling the 575-m lateral, water production index fell to about 14% of baseline and dimensionless water-oil ratio was down 78% vs pre-trial; oil production could increase 2.6 times at the same liquid rate (authors caution the before/after comparison). [source](https://www.ogj.com/drilling-production/production-operations/article/17232592/continuous-pack-off-inflow-control-device-lowers-water-cut-in-trial-offshore-china)
- water cut with optimised inflow control valves: 30 % water cut; Sector model of an Iranian field: water cut 80% in conventional wells against 30% with optimised ICVs; cumulative water 14.0 to 3.5 million bbl.. [source](https://ijogst.put.ac.ir/article_13827.html)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Long subsea tiebacks with direct electrical heating
<a id="ups-direct-electrical-heating-tiebacks"></a>

Alternating current is passed through the pipeline wall to keep fluids above hydrate temperature or to re-heat them after a shutdown. Tyrihans is the longest DEH line described, 43 km, 18 inch, about 10 MW, with Huldra (16 km) before it.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PWR["Supply AC via piggyback cable<br/><small>power cable, terminations</small>"]
    HEAT["Current heats the steel wall<br/><small>pipeline</small>"]
    MON["Monitor temperature<br/><small>temperature sensors</small>"]
    PWR --> HEAT
    HEAT --> MON
```

**Key equipment:** power cable, pipeline terminations and anodes, topside power unit.

**Performance:**
- Tyrihans DEH tieback length: 43 km (18 in); about 10 MW. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/pipelines/article/16760932/direct-heating-safeguards-flow-through-harsh-environment-pipelines)
- Ormen Lange DEH retrofit power: 6.4 MW (2.6 kA, 52 kV); 20 km section, 30 in. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/pipelines/article/16760932/direct-heating-safeguards-flow-through-harsh-environment-pipelines)
- longest electrically trace-heated (ETH) pipe-in-pipe production pipeline (Fenja tieback, for comparison with DEH): 37 km; 37-km ETH pipe-in-pipe pipeline for Neptune Energy's Fenja field installed and tested in 2020-21 campaigns, claimed by Neptune as the world's longest to date for that technology; tieback to Equinor's Njord A platform — a different active-heating technology from DEH. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/14208015/record-length-heated-subsea-production-pipeline-installed-at-fenja-oil-field-offshore-norway)
- longest DEH-heated subsea flowline: 43 km; Lianzi field: 43 km DEH flowline in 1,050 m of water, the longest and deepest DEH installation; Tyrihans holds the highest voltage at 52 kV.. [source](https://blog.sintef.com/energy/electrical-heating-flow-assurance-subsea-flowlines)

**Reference plants:**
- Tyrihans DEH tieback to the Kristin FPSO (Norwegian Sea), NO: 43 km, 18-in production pipeline with piggybacked DEH cable, about 10 MW, 52 kV cable — the longest DEH line described in the source; operating. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/pipelines/article/16760932/direct-heating-safeguards-flow-through-harsh-environment-pipelines)
- Huldra DEH pipeline (North Sea), NO: 16 km DEH piggybacked pipeline; activated about 60 times since commissioning in May 2002 — first commercial DEH use in the source; operating. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/pipelines/article/16760932/direct-heating-safeguards-flow-through-harsh-environment-pipelines)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (direct electrical heating subsea pipeline cost per km); only paywalled OnePetro papers and OEM (ABB, SLB OneSubsea) product pages found, no open cost figure. ## Managed pressure drilling
<a id="ups-managed-pressure-drilling"></a>

IADC defines MPD as an adaptive drilling process that precisely controls the annular pressure profile; a rotating control device and automated choke let the driller hold bottomhole pressure within a narrow window. Variants include continuous circulation, constant bottomhole pressure, pressurised mud cap and dual-gradient drilling.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SEAL["Seal annulus with a rotating control device<br/><small>RCD</small>"]
    CTRL["Control back-pressure with automated choke<br/><small>PLC choke manifold, mass-flow meter</small>"]
    DRILL["Drill within the pore-fracture window<br/><small>rig</small>"]
    SEAL --> CTRL
    CTRL --> DRILL
```

**Key equipment:** rotating control device (API 16RCD), automated choke manifold, mass-flow meter, non-return valves.

**Performance:**
- marine RCD tensile rating when placed below the riser tension ring: 3e+06 lb; typical. [source](https://iadc.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/08/preview-mpub.pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- Petrobras managed-pressure drilling programme, offshore Brazil pre-salt, BR: 2 rigs equipped for MPD as of mid-2014, planned expansion to 12 by end-2015 and 16 by 2016; seven wells drilled with MPD at the time; operating. [source](https://jpt.spe.org/managed-pressure-drilling-migrates-deep-water-offshore-brazil)

**Missing inputs:**
- cost versus conventional drilling (costs): The preview gives none. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open published MPD-vs-conventional cost comparison (SPE papers restricted). Where: IADC MPD publications (open). Next: Add a published cost or time saving.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched managed pressure, MPD, underbalanced, drilling); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Sources: [1](https://iadc.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/08/preview-mpub.pdf), [2](https://jpt.spe.org/managed-pressure-drilling-migrates-deep-water-offshore-brazil), [3](https://www.drillopedia.com/mpd-equipment). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Measurement and logging while drilling (MWD/LWD)
<a id="ups-mwd-lwd"></a>

Sensors in the bottom-hole assembly measure well direction (MWD) and formation properties (LWD) while drilling and send data to the surface, usually by mud pulses, for real-time geosteering. LWD measurements include gamma ray, resistivity, density, neutron porosity, sonic, NMR, images and formation testing.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SENSE["Sensors measure direction and formation<br/><small>MWD and LWD collars</small>"]
    TX["Transmit by mud pulse or store in memory<br/><small>mud pulser</small>"]
    STEER["Geosteer to stay in the target zone<br/><small>geosteering software</small>"]
    SENSE --> TX
    TX --> STEER
```

**Key equipment:** MWD tool, LWD collars, mud pulser, surface decoding.

**Performance:**
- mud-pulse telemetry data rate: 3-20 bit/s; Up to 20 bit/s against below 3 bit/s conventionally, including 15 bit/s at 24,000 ft in the North Sea.. [source](https://www.academia.edu/9840372/IADC_SPE_112683_A_New_Mud_Pulse_Telemetry_System_for_Enhanced_MWD_LWD_Applications)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Methane leak detection and repair (LDAR, satellites and continuous monitoring)
<a id="ups-methane-leak-detection"></a>

Leaks are located by surveying facilities with optical gas imaging cameras, aircraft or drone sensors, fixed continuous sensors or satellites, and then quantified and repaired. Satellites flag large emission events and recurrent leaks, which are passed to operators for rapid repair. A repeat survey confirms the fix.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DET["Detection<br/><small>optical gas imaging cameras, aircraft and drones, continuous point sensors, satellites</small>"]
    QNT["Quantification and attribution<br/><small>emission quantification and facility mapping</small>"]
    REP["Repair or replacement<br/><small>maintenance crews, replacement of leaking equipment</small>"]
    VER["Verification and reporting<br/><small>repeat survey, measurement-based reporting</small>"]
    DET --> QNT
    QNT --> REP
    REP --> VER
    VER --> DET
```

**Key equipment:** optical gas imaging cameras, continuous methane sensors, aircraft and drone payloads, satellite data feeds, repair crews and replacement equipment.

**Performance:**
- fossil-fuel methane emissions that could be avoided at no net cost: 30 % of 2024 fossil-fuel methane emissions; about 35 Mt; at average 2024 energy prices; oil and gas share higher than coal. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- methane emitted by the fossil fuel sector: 200 bcm in 2024; abatement could have made nearly 100 bcm of gas available. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- satellites in orbit able to observe methane emissions: 25 satellites (more than); MethaneSAT and Tanager-1 started in 2024. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- share of satellite-detected oil and gas sources that were recurrent: 25 % (about); Carbon Mapper analysis of more than 2,000 plumes. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)

**Costs:**
- greenhouse gas emissions price at which almost all available methane abatement measures across the energy sector would be cost-effective: 20 USD/tCO2-eq (2025$); dollar year not stated; read from the marginal abatement cost curves for methane from the fossil fuel sector, 2024; 1 t methane treated as 30 t CO2-eq (GWP100, IPCC 2021); value of captured methane based on 2024 net-back natural gas import prices; per-survey and per-sensor costs are not stated in the collected documents. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- payback period of many oil and gas methane abatement options: 1 years; dollar year not stated; document states many abatement options can pay for themselves within a year and around 30% of oil and gas industry emissions could be avoided with measures offering rates of return of more than 25%; value of methane captured based on net-back natural gas import prices in 2024. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)
- share of LDAR emissions savings achievable at no net cost to operators (Eurasia oil and gas): 95 % of LDAR emissions savings in Eurasia; dollar year not stated; includes rapid-response systems for addressing large emissions events detected by satellite. [source](https://iea.blob.core.windows.net/assets/b83c32dd-fc1b-4917-96e9-8cd918801cbf/GlobalMethaneTracker2025.pdf)

TRL source: [IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide, dataset of 24 July 2026, 2025 assessment (IEA 1-11 scale): 'Periodic methane emission monitoring in oil and gas facilities' 9; 'Satellite-based methane emission monitoring in oil and gas facilities' 9](https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/data-tools/etp-clean-energy-technology-guide)

**Missing inputs:**
- regional (Southeast Asia) service providers (suppliers): No source read names providers in Malaysia or Indonesia. Web-checked 2026-10-28; the named LDAR providers found (Encino, Applus, Gazomat) are US/EU/Middle-East; no SEA-local provider with an open page found; card already lists satellite operators (GHGSat, Kayrros) that cover the region. Where: PETRONAS and Pertamina methane programmes (catalog: petronas-tech, open). Next: Add regional operators or providers.

## Minimum-facility wellhead platform structures and marine systems
<a id="ups-whp-platform-structures-and-marine-systems"></a>

Minimum-facility wellhead platform structures and marine systems: jackets, topsides, risers/J-tubes, helidecks, jack-up installation and unmanned operation. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    JACKET_STRUCTURE["Jacket structure<br/><small>Jacket structure</small>"]
    TOPSIDE_DECKS["Topside decks<br/><small>Topside decks</small>"]
    RISERS_AND_J_TUBES["Risers and J-tubes for pipelines and subsea cables<br/><small>Risers and J-tubes for pipelines and subsea cables</small>"]
    HELIDECK["Helideck<br/><small>Helideck</small>"]
    MINIMUM_FACILITY_L["Minimum-facility WHP logistics: designed for jack-up-rig installation, little or<br/><small>Minimum-facility WHP logistics: designed for jack-up-rig installation, little or</small>"]
    MALAYSIAN_OPERATIN["Malaysian WHP operating context: normally unmanned, no separators/produced-water<br/><small>Malaysian WHP operating context: normally unmanned, no separators/produced-water</small>"]
    JACKET_STRUCTURE --> TOPSIDE_DECKS
    TOPSIDE_DECKS --> RISERS_AND_J_TUBES
    RISERS_AND_J_TUBES --> HELIDECK
    HELIDECK --> MINIMUM_FACILITY_L
    MINIMUM_FACILITY_L --> MALAYSIAN_OPERATIN
```

**Key equipment:** Jacket structure, Topside decks, Risers and J-tubes for pipelines and subsea cables, Helideck, Minimum-facility WHP logistics: designed for jack-up-rig installation, little or no onboard, Malaysian WHP operating context: normally unmanned, no separators/produced-water treatment/g.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — Survival load case recurrence period: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 8.2: survival state under wave/current/wind attack with 50 or 100 years recurrence period.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Operational load case recurrence period: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 8.2: operational state under wave/current/wind attack with 1 or 5 years recurrence period, under full operation.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Offshore inspection, maintenance and repair share of OPEX: 20 % of OPEX (North Sea); ESDEP 15A.1 section 9.3: "In the North Sea approximately 20 percent of OPEX are required for offshore inspection, maintenance and repair (IMR)".. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — IMR expenditure over project life: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 9.3: amount spent on IMR over project life "can add up to approximately half the original investment".. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Dynamic analysis trigger: 3 seconds (natural period); ESDEP 15A.1 section 8.1: dynamic analysis performed for system behaviour under wave attack if the natural period exceeds 3 seconds.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Fatigue assessment inclusion: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 8.2 lists fatigue assessment alongside survival, operational and accidental cases; DNV-RP-C203 objective is methods for fatigue assessment of offshore steel structures.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US4932811A/en)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/kebabangan-field)
- Reliability — GBS topside rigid-connection fatigue: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 5.3.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Local dynamic behaviour items: no range recorded; ESDEP 15A.1 section 8.1.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)
- Reliability — Design life of Tarpon KMSE/AJK WHPs: 20 years (operational lifespan, design); Tarpon Systems project page: platforms designed for a 20-year operational lifespan and made relocatable.. [source](https://www.tarponsystems.com/installation-of-two-wellhead-platforms-for-kayu-manis-south-east-and-anjung-kecil)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://fgg-web.fgg.uni-lj.si/~/pmoze/esdep/master/wg15a/l0100.htm)

- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/anjung-kecil-oil-field)
- Reliability — Conductor contribution to total environmental loading on fixed platform: no range recorded; US5513929A background: "well conductors above the seabed contribute a major portion of the total environmental loading on the platform (typically twenty to 30 percent)".. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US5513929A/en)

## Multiphase flow metering
<a id="ups-multiphase-flow-metering"></a>

Meters measure oil, water and gas rates without separation. Emerson's Roxar 2600 combines a Venturi differential-pressure measurement with electrical impedance and optional gamma densitometry, reports at 10 Hz and covers 0 to 100% water-liquid ratio and 0 to 100% gas volume fraction.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DP["Venturi measures total mass flow<br/><small>Venturi</small>"]
    IMP["Impedance and gamma give phase fractions<br/><small>electrodes, gamma densitometer</small>"]
    CALC["Software computes phase rates<br/><small>flow computer</small>"]
    DP --> IMP
    IMP --> CALC
```

**Key equipment:** Venturi, impedance sensors, gamma densitometer, flow computer.

**Performance:**
- Roxar 2600 measurement frequency: 10 Hz; phase rates in real time. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en-us/automation/measurement-instrumentation/flow-measurement/multiphase-meters)
- water-liquid ratio range: 0-100 %; WLR. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en-us/automation/measurement-instrumentation/flow-measurement/multiphase-meters)
- gas volume fraction range: 0-100 %; GVF. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en-us/automation/measurement-instrumentation/flow-measurement/multiphase-meters)
- multiphase flow meter measurement uncertainty: 5-10 % of reading; Allocation agreements required 5% on oil and gas and 10% on water; the meter was verified to 5% on oil and gas over six months at 80-85% GVF.. [source](https://www.osti.gov/etdeweb/servlets/purl/20892519)

**Reference plants:**
- Kashagan MPFM deployment (NCOC/Schlumberger), Kashagan field, Kazakhstan, KZ: multiphase flow meters on unmanned production islands: one MPFM per group of wells on some islands, one per well on others; used for allocation, well testing and virtual-meter calibration; operating. [source](https://www.academia.edu/66524444/Multiphase_flow_metering_Current_trends_and_future_developments)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; meter prices are vendor-quoted only. ## Offshore platform electrification (power from shore, offshore wind)
<a id="ups-platform-electrification"></a>

Platforms take electricity from shore or from offshore wind instead of burning gas on site. Johan Sverdrup runs on hydro power from shore at about 0.67 kg CO2 per barrel against a world average of 15 kg, and Hywind Tampen's 94.6 MW meets about 35% of five platforms' demand.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SRC["Supply power from shore cable or floating wind<br/><small>HVAC or HVDC cable, floating wind turbines</small>"]
    PLAT["Distribute to platform loads<br/><small>switchgear, transformers</small>"]
    SRC --> PLAT
```

**Key equipment:** power cable, converter or transformer, platform switchgear, floating wind turbines (Hywind).

**Performance:**
- Johan Sverdrup CO2 intensity: 0.67 kg CO2/bbl; versus 15 kg world average. [source](https://www.equinor.com/energy/johan-sverdrup)
- Johan Sverdrup plateau capacity: 755000 bbl/d; Phase 1 Oct 2019, Phase 2 Dec 2022. [source](https://www.equinor.com/energy/johan-sverdrup)
- Hywind Tampen capacity: 94.6 MW; 11 turbines of 8.6 MW. [source](https://www.equinor.com/energy/hywind-tampen)

**Costs:**
- Contracted day rates, next-generation wind turbine installation vessels (WTIVs) and heavy-lift jack-ups: 200000-350000 USD/day; Contracted dayrates for major project campaigns for Cadeler Wind Peaks/Wind Apex, Heerema Aegir, Charybdis, Van Oord Boreas, DEME Orion/Gulliver; attributed to genuine scarcity of suitable installation capacity.  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://offshoreindustry.co.uk/offshore-heavy-lift-2026-crane-vessel-scarcity-sscv-market-dynamics-and-the-wind-sector-collision/)
- Jack-up rig one-well option contract value, ~30 days: 4e+06 USD; TotalEnergies exercised a one-well option for HDHE jack-up Valaris Stavanger in UK North Sea, ~30 additional days.  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://www.rivieramm.com/news-content-hub/north-seas-jack-up-pool-shrinks-as-rigs-move-to-more-lucrative-waters-80654)
- Jack-up rig contract value, 330-day campaign (including minor rig modifications): 4.8e+07 USD; TotalEnergies / VALARIS Stavanger 330-day campaign from March 2024; approximate total contract value as reported.  Dollar year not stated.. [source](https://www.rivieramm.com/news-content-hub/rigs-report-north-sea-jack-up-utility-hits-94-79737)
- Valaris heavy-duty harsh-environment (HDHE) jack-up average day rate, 2024: 125000 USD/day (2024$); Average day rates as of 29 July 2024, excluding non-recurring mobilisation/contract-preparation revenue; rigs used in the North Sea.. [source](https://s23.q4cdn.com/956522167/files/doc_downloads/2024/07292024-Fleet-Status-Report_FINAL.pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- Johan Sverdrup power-from-shore (Equinor), North Sea, NO: field producing since Oct 2019 entirely on hydropower from shore; CO2 emissions 0.67 kg/bbl vs about 9 kg/bbl NCS average; launch estimate 460,000 t CO2/yr avoided, Equinor now states more than 620,000 t/yr; operating. [source](https://www.equinor.com/energy/electrification)

**Missing inputs:**
- electrification capex per platform with year (costs): Neither page gives costs. Web-checked 2026-10-28; Equinor pages state emission figures, not per-platform capex. Where: Norwegian Offshore Directorate reports (catalog: norway-sodir, open). Next: Add a cost with year.

IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched platform electrification, electrification of offshore, electrification, offshore platform); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). ## Oil sands mining and froth treatment
<a id="ups-oil-sands-mining"></a>

Oil sand is mined in open pits, crushed and mixed with 50 to 80 °C water so bitumen floats as froth (about 60% bitumen, 30% water, 10% solids), then diluent and settlers clean it. About two tons of sand yield a barrel, and tailings are the main environmental problem.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MINE["Open-pit mining<br/><small>shovels, trucks</small>"]
    EXT["Hot-water extraction and flotation<br/><small>slurry pipeline, separation vessel</small>"]
    FROTH["Froth treatment<br/><small>inclined plate settlers, centrifuges</small>"]
    MINE --> EXT
    EXT --> FROTH
```

**Key equipment:** shovels and trucks, crushers, separation vessels, froth treatment plant, tailings ponds.

**Performance:**
- bitumen recovery: 90-100 %; modern hot-water extraction. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_oil_sands)
- natural gas use, mining with upgrading: 1.54 GJ per barrel; CERI figure via Wikipedia, year not stated. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_oil_sands)
- mined share of oil sands production, 2010: 53 %; in situ 47%. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_oil_sands)
- fluid tailings inventory reduction, Suncor Base Mine: 17 %; Inventory fell from 316 million m3 in 2015 to 263 million m3 in 2019, about 17%.. [source](https://www.abenergycentre.ca/oil/notable-acceleration-in-oil-sands-tailings-tech-development-paying-off-with-results)

**Reference plants:**
- Muskeg River Mine (Albian Sands Energy, Shell-led), Athabasca, Alberta, CA: full production 155,000 b/d of bitumen (first oil delayed by a Jan 2003 fire, ramp-up during 2003); operating. [source](https://www.ogj.com/drilling-production/article/17255656/fire-at-muskeg-river-mine-delays-first-athabasca-bitumen-production)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found.
