## Plunger lift and hydraulic jet pumps
<a id="ups-plunger-hydraulic-lift"></a>

Plunger lift uses the well's own gas pressure to push a plunger and liquid slug up the tubing; hydraulic pumps (piston, jet) are driven by power fluid from the surface. Jet pumps tolerate solids above 50% by volume and hydraulic pumps cover about 1,000 to 18,000 ft.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    BUILD["Gas pressure builds under the plunger<br/><small>plunger, lubricator</small>"]
    RISE["Plunger lifts the liquid slug"]
    FLOW["Well flows; plunger falls and cycle repeats<br/><small>controller</small>"]
    BUILD --> RISE
    RISE --> FLOW
```

**Key equipment:** plunger, lubricator, controller, hydraulic jet pump and power-fluid system.

**Performance:**
- hydraulic pump depth range: 1000-18000 ft; piston, jet, turbine. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- jet pump solids tolerance: 50 % volume (above); hydraulic pumps, Wikipedia comparison table. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- plunger lift and hydraulic pump application envelopes (rate and depth): 25-4000 blpd (hydraulic pump 25-4,000; plunger lift 1-100 vertical / 1-400 horizontal bbl per MMscfd gas, <5,000 ft TVD); IPIECA Energy Efficiency Compendium artificial-lift table: plunger lift <5,000 ft TVD with liquid rate 1-100 (vertical) / 1-400 (horizontal) bbl per MMscfd of gas, no external power required except valve actuation; linear hydraulic pump 25-4,000 blpd, <15,000 ft TVD (jet/venturi pumps discussed under hydraulic pumping). [source](https://www.ipieca.org/resources/energy-efficiency-compendium/artificial-lift-2023)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). ## Produced water treatment (hydrocyclones, flotation, membranes)
<a id="ups-produced-water-treatment"></a>

Water produced with oil is treated by gravity separators, hydrocyclones, plate coalescers, dissolved gas flotation and nutshell filters before discharge or reinjection. Radioactive radium in some brines (above 3,500 pCi/L in one sample) adds disposal constraints; membranes are not covered by the page read.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    GRAV["Gravity separation and coalescing<br/><small>separator, plate coalescer</small>"]
    HYC["Hydrocyclones and flotation<br/><small>hydrocyclones, flotation unit</small>"]
    POL["Polishing filters, then discharge or reinjection<br/><small>nutshell filters</small>"]
    GRAV --> HYC
    HYC --> POL
```

**Key equipment:** hydrocyclones, flotation unit, filters, reinjection pumps.

**Performance:**
- radium in an Ohio brine sample: 3500 pCi/L (above); NRC discharge limit below 60 pCi/L for Ra-226 and Ra-228. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Produced_water)
- OSPAR dispersed-oil-in-produced-water discharge performance standard (NE Atlantic offshore): 30 mg oil per litre discharged (average annual performance standard); OSPAR offshore discharges report tables list "installations for which the average annual oil content of the produced water discharged to the sea exceeds the 30 mg/l performance standard"; treatment to below the standard is by hydrocyclones/flotation, for which the sources read state no single efficiency figure. [source](https://oap.ospar.org/media/filer_public/54/79/54796902-37e5-4166-816c-5177bf65b584/p00634e_offshore_discharges_report_2012.pdf)
- oil-in-water after treatment: 10-30 mg/L; typical discharge compliance range. [source](https://www.frames-group.com/)

**Reference plants:**
- Pengerang Integrated Complex effluent systems: ; . [source](https://www.petronas.com/)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Production optimisation and digital oilfield
<a id="ups-production-optimisation-digital-oilfield"></a>

Real-time data from wells and facilities feed models that recommend or set choke, lift and process settings, often from a remote operations centre. ADNOC and SLB report an AI-enabled real-time operations centre covering more than 120 rigs, with human effort down 30 to 40%; these are company claims relayed by a vendor article.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DATA["Collect sensor and SCADA data<br/><small>sensors, SCADA</small>"]
    MODEL["Models diagnose and recommend<br/><small>analytics platform</small>"]
    ACT["Operators or controls apply changes<br/><small>remote operations centre</small>"]
    DATA --> MODEL
    MODEL --> ACT
```

**Key equipment:** sensors, data platform, analytics software, remote operations centre.

**Performance:**
- human effort reduction at ADNOC rig operations centre: 30-40 %; ADNOC statement relayed by AVEVA. [source](https://www.aveva.com/en/our-industrial-life/type/article/when-the-rig-starts-thinking-the-rise-of-autonomous-drilling/)
- production increase from digital oilfield implementation: 2-8 %; IHS CERA: 2-8% production increase and 5-25% operating expense reduction.. [source](https://www.imeche.org/news/news-article/digital-oilfield-2.0-uses-smart-technology-to-boost-production-efficiency)

## Reservoir simulation (black oil, compositional, thermal)
<a id="ups-reservoir-simulation"></a>

Numerical models solve mass conservation, phase behaviour and Darcy flow on a grid to forecast production and test recovery schemes. Black-oil models track oil, water and gas; compositional models track components with an equation of state; thermal models add energy balance for heavy oil.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MODEL["Build static grid and rock-fluid properties<br/><small>geological model, PVT data</small>"]
    HM["History match to production and pressure<br/><small>simulator</small>"]
    FORE["Forecast scenarios<br/><small>simulator</small>"]
    MODEL --> HM
    HM --> FORE
```

**Key equipment:** reservoir simulator, HPC cluster, PVT and SCAL data, history-matching tools.

**Performance:**
- runtime for a 2-billion-cell simulation: 8 hours; GigaPOWERS V9 ran a 2-billion-cell CO2 storage model simulating 1,000 years in 8 hours.. [source](https://www.aramcolife.com/en/publications/the-arabian-sun/articles/2023/week-33-articles/gigapowers-v9-celebrated)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. ## Rod lift and progressing cavity pumps
<a id="ups-rod-lift-pcp"></a>

A surface pumping unit moves a sucker-rod string that drives a downhole plunger pump (rod lift), or a rotating rod string drives a progressing cavity pump (PCP). PCPs reach about 1,800 m and 5,000 bbl/d, and aromatics can degrade the elastomer stator.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRV["Surface unit or rotary drive<br/><small>pumping unit or PCP drive head</small>"]
    ROD["Rod string transmits motion<br/><small>sucker rods</small>"]
    PUMP["Downhole pump lifts fluid<br/><small>plunger pump or PCP</small>"]
    DRV --> ROD
    ROD --> PUMP
```

**Key equipment:** pumping unit, sucker rods, downhole pump, PCP stator and rotor.

**Performance:**
- PCP depth capability: 1800 m (6,000 ft); up to 5,000 bbl/day. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- rod pump realistic output vs theoretical: 80 %; of theoretical output. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_lift)
- rod lift and PCP application envelopes (rate and depth): 100-10000 ft TVD (sucker rod pump 100-10,000; liquid rate up to 5,000 blpd); IPIECA Energy Efficiency Compendium artificial-lift table: sucker rod pump 5,000 blpd, 100-10,000 ft TVD; rod-driven elastomeric PCP 5-5,000 blpd, 100-9,000 ft; rod-driven metal-to-metal PCP 350-2,500 blpd, 100-3,500 ft; electric submersible PCP 5-5,000 blpd, 100-8,000 ft. [source](https://www.ipieca.org/resources/energy-efficiency-compendium/artificial-lift-2023)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Rotary steerable systems
<a id="ups-rotary-steerable-systems"></a>

Downhole tools steer the bit while the whole string rotates, either by pushing pads against the wellbore (push-the-bit) or by bending the shaft inside the tool (point-the-bit). Continuous rotation improves cuttings transport and weight transfer and produces a smoother borehole than sliding with a mud motor.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRILL["Rotate string from surface<br/><small>top drive</small>"]
    STEER["Tool adjusts bit direction downhole<br/><small>push or point-the-bit RSS</small>"]
    SURV["Survey and send commands by mud pulse<br/><small>MWD</small>"]
    DRILL --> STEER
    STEER --> SURV
```

**Key equipment:** rotary steerable tool, MWD tools, top drive, mud pumps.

**Performance:**
- rotary-steerable build (dogleg) capability, PowerDrive suite: 8 deg/100 ft dogleg severity (maximum); "[Schlumberger] PowerDrive suite of fully rotating rotary steerable systems ... can develop dogleg severity as high as 8 deg/100 ft, significantly higher than" bent-motor practice; ROP gain and service day rates not stated in open sources. [source](https://drillingcontractor.org/dcpi/2004/dc-septoct04/Sep4-Schlumberger.pdf)
- rate-of-penetration improvement with a high-build RSS: 50-170 % ROP; Woodford 80% faster with 10 days saved, Eagle Ford 85%, Marcellus 170%, Thailand 50%; build rates 8-12 deg/100 ft against 6-8 typical.. [source](https://jpt.spe.org/hybrid-rotary-steerable-system-delivers-higher-build-rates-and-smoother-holes)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; service day rates are vendor-quoted only. ## Sand control (gravel pack, frac pack, screens)
<a id="ups-sand-control"></a>

Sand control keeps formation sand out of the well in soft reservoirs, typically with compressive strength below about 1,000 psi, using gravel packs, frac packs or screens; excluding sand builds a filter cake that can impair productivity, while managed sand production is sometimes cheaper and recovers more oil. PetroWiki's page covers the managed approach, not the mechanical methods.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SCREEN["Run screens across the interval<br/><small>sand screens</small>"]
    PACK["Place gravel pack or frac pack<br/><small>gravel slurry, pumping unit</small>"]
    FLOW["Produce through the filter"]
    SCREEN --> PACK
    PACK --> FLOW
```

**Key equipment:** sand screens, gravel, packers, pumping equipment.

**Performance:**
- soft-formation compressive strength threshold: 1000 psi (under); typical definition. [source](https://petrowiki.spe.org/Sand_control)

**Missing inputs:**
- frac-pack procedure, premium and expandable screen types, and the pumping/TSO sequence (process): The gravel-pack sequence is now cited (SPE PetroWiki), which covers only two screen types and no frac packing; those parts remain open. Where: SPE PetroWiki sand control sections (catalog: onepetro, open). Next: Leave open until an operator or equipment source publishes this detail.
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Seawater sulfate removal for injection water
<a id="ups-seawater-sulfate-removal"></a>

Nanofiltration membranes strip sulfate from seawater before injection so it does not form barium sulfate scale with reservoir water. At Marathon's Brae field the first plant (South Brae, 1988) handled 40,000 bw/d with sulfate rejection of 98 to 99% and about 75% feed recovery.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    FILT["Pre-filter seawater<br/><small>filters</small>"]
    NF["Nanofiltration removes sulfate<br/><small>spiral-wound membranes</small>"]
    INJ["Inject low-sulfate water<br/><small>injection pumps</small>"]
    FILT --> NF
    NF --> INJ
```

**Key equipment:** nanofiltration membranes, pre-filtration, antiscalant dosing, high-pressure pumps.

**Performance:**
- sulfate rejection: 98-99 %; first and second generation membranes. [source](https://www.ogj.com/general-interest/companies/article/17235495/membranes-solve-north-sea-waterflood-sulfate-problems)
- feed-water recovery: 75 %; about. [source](https://www.ogj.com/general-interest/companies/article/17235495/membranes-solve-north-sea-waterflood-sulfate-problems)
- membrane life: 4-5 years; chemical cleaning at pH 3 to 10. [source](https://www.ogj.com/general-interest/companies/article/17235495/membranes-solve-north-sea-waterflood-sulfate-problems)

**Reference plants:**
- Sulfate Removal Package (Veolia SRP with Dow FilmTec nanofiltration membranes), offshore Angola, AO: seawater treatment for injection: SRP removes sulphates and other divalent ions from injection water (capacity not stated in the source); operating. [source](https://www.pollutionsolutions-online.com/news/water-wastewater/17/veolia-water-solutions-technologies/ultrafiltration-and-sulphate-removal-seawater-treatment-in-offshore-angola/14681)

**Missing inputs:**
- capex and opex with year (costs): Not stated. Web-checked 2026-10-28; package prices are vendor-quoted only. Where: Veolia and DuPont case studies (open). Next: Add cost per m3 with year.

IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched sulfate, sulphate, nanofiltration, desalination, water treatment); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Shale oil and gas multi-stage horizontal development
<a id="ups-shale-multistage-horizontal"></a>

Horizontal wells with laterals of 1,500 to 10,000 ft are fractured in 30 or more stages to produce tight oil and shale gas. EIA data cited by Wikipedia put US tight oil at 7.23 million b/d in 2021, about 64% of US crude.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRILL["Drill horizontal lateral<br/><small>rotary steerable system</small>"]
    FRAC["Multi-stage hydraulic fracturing<br/><small>frac fleet</small>"]
    FLOW["Flowback and production<br/><small>separators</small>"]
    DRILL --> FRAC
    FRAC --> FLOW
```

**Key equipment:** rig, frac fleet, proppant and water supply, flowback equipment.

**Performance:**
- US tight oil production, 2021: 7.23 million b/d; about 64% of US crude oil. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_oil)
- lateral length: 1500-10000 ft; Barnett 1,500 to 5,000; Bakken up to 10,000. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydraulic_fracturing)

**Missing inputs:**
- well cost and decline rate with year (costs): Not given; the collected EIA document (AEO2026 Electricity Market Module assumptions, April 2026) has no shale well-cost or decline-rate data (it covers power-plant technology costs only). Web-checked 2026-10-28: open academic sources give Sichuan-basin D&C at USD 10.4-11.7 m/well (mid-2015) and scattered Marcellus averages, but no current authoritative US well-cost series in open form; EIA discontinued the DPR well-cost content. Where: EIA drilling productivity report (catalog: eia-reports, open). Next: Add cost and decline figures.

Web-checked 2026-10-28; play-level counts only, no named verifiable well set on open pages. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). ## Subsea boosting and multiphase pumps
<a id="ups-subsea-boosting"></a>

Pumps on the seabed add pressure to the multiphase stream to raise flow and recovery. Pazflor (800 m, 2011) uses six pumps plus two spares at 13.8 MW with up to 105 bar differential; Draugen (1994) was the first, at 1 MW and 53 bar.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IN["Wellstream enters pump station<br/><small>subsea manifold</small>"]
    PUMP["Helico-axial or twin-screw pump adds pressure<br/><small>multiphase pump, motor</small>"]
    OUT["Boosted flow to host<br/><small>flowline</small>"]
    IN --> PUMP
    PUMP --> OUT
```

**Key equipment:** multiphase pumps, electric motors, power and control umbilicals, subsea controls, multiphase pump, subsea separator, wet-gas meter, electric choke.

**Performance:**
- Pazflor boost differential pressure: 105 bar (max); 13.8 MW, 800 m, 2011. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/458098-deepwater-production-gets-high-pressure-boost)
- Barracuda pump output gain: 40 % over natural flow; 1,000 m3/d, 60 bar operating. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/458098-deepwater-production-gets-high-pressure-boost)
- Draugen first subsea multiphase pump gain: 5000 b/d; 1 MW, 53 bar, 6 km tieback, 1994. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/458098-deepwater-production-gets-high-pressure-boost)

**Reference plants:**
- Perdido subsea boosting (Baker Hughes), Gulf of Mexico, US: subsea pumps boosting production from three satellite fields (Great White, Silvertip, Tobago) tied back to the Perdido spar; installed 2010; operating. [source](http://www.findingpetroleum.com/n/Baker-Hughes-installs-subsea-pumps-at-Perdido-field/8c0b00f8.aspx)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Subsea gas compression
<a id="ups-subsea-gas-compression"></a>

Compressors on the seabed boost gas to the platform, replacing topside compression. Equinor's Åsgard system, in about 300 m of water with a 40 km pipeline to Åsgard B, raised recovery on Midgard from 67% to 87% and on Mikkel from 59% to 84%, adding 306 million boe for just above NOK 19 billion.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SEP["Gas-liquid separation<br/><small>scrubber</small>"]
    COMP["Compress gas<br/><small>subsea compressor, motor</small>"]
    EXP["Export gas by pipeline<br/><small>pipeline</small>"]
    SEP --> COMP
    COMP --> EXP
```

**Key equipment:** subsea compressor trains, scrubbers, power and control umbilicals.

**Performance:**
- Midgard recovery: 67-87 % (before to after); Equinor, 17 September 2015. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/archive/2015/09/17/17SepAasgardsubsea)
- Mikkel recovery: 59-84 % (before to after); Mikkel field recovery factor before and after subsea compression, as stated in the Equinor news item. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/archive/2015/09/17/17SepAasgardsubsea)
- added reserves: 306 million boe; overall. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/archive/2015/09/17/17SepAasgardsubsea)
- Asgard subsea gas compression duty and availability: 11.5 MW per compressor unit (two HOFIM motor-compressor units); availability approaching 100% over 80,000 operational hours; "an availability approaching 100%" after 80,000 operational hours since September 2015; two MAN Energy Solutions HOFIM motor-compressor units, each 11.5 MW, at 300 m water depth. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/14185923/asgard-subsea-compressors-provide-high-availability)

**Costs:**
- project cost: 19 NOK billion (just above); dollar year not stated. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/archive/2015/09/17/17SepAasgardsubsea)

**Reference plants:**
- Asgard subsea gas compression, Norwegian Sea, NO: world's first subsea gas compression facility; on stream 17 Sep 2015; phase 2 started 2025 (first module replaced 2023, second installed at 270 m); recovery from Mikkel and Midgard rising to 90%, about 306 million boe additional, about NOK 175 bn added value; operating. [source](https://www.equinor.com/news/20250926-phase-2-asgard-subsea-compression)

- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched subsea compression, subsea, compressor, wet gas); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Subsea production systems (trees, manifolds, umbilicals)
<a id="ups-subsea-production-systems"></a>

Wells are completed on the seabed with trees, linked by manifolds, jumpers, umbilicals and flowlines to a host. TechnipFMC delivered its 100th standardised Subsea 2.0 tree in May 2025 with lead times about a third shorter than engineered-to-order trees; Westwood counted 255 tree awards in 2024.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    TREE["Install tree on wellhead<br/><small>subsea tree</small>"]
    MAN["Gather via manifold and flowlines<br/><small>manifold, jumpers</small>"]
    HOST["Control and power via umbilical<br/><small>umbilical, control system</small>"]
    TREE --> MAN
    MAN --> HOST
```

**Key equipment:** subsea trees, manifolds, umbilicals, control systems, flowlines, subsea tree, subsea wellhead, manifold/PLET, umbilical and SCM, flexible flowline, SCR, subsea boosting, CALM/SPM export, riser, subsea umbilical, pipeline/flowline, wet-mate connector, BOP, subsea separator, injection system.

**Performance:**
- subsea tree awards in 2024: 255 units; 12% below 2023. [source](https://www.westwoodenergy.com/sectors/offshore-energy-services/subsea-equipment-and-services/global-subsea-tree-tracker-2025)
- Subsea 2.0 lead-time reduction: 33 % (about one third); versus engineered-to-order trees; vendor claim. [source](https://www.technipfmc.com/en/media/news/2025/05/technipfmc-marks-milestone-100th-subsea-20-tree-delivery-with-client-that-placed-first-order/)
- world water-depth records for subsea completions (Gulf of Mexico Perdido development): 2851-2925 m water depth (Silvertip 9,356 ft = 2,851 m record Dec 2008; Tobago about 2,925 m world's deepest subsea completion); Shell set a world water-depth record completing a subsea well 9,356 ft below sea level at Silvertip (OGJ, 3 Dec 2008); Tobago, in roughly 2,925 m of water, is the world's deepest subsea completion (Offshore Magazine, 1 Dec 2010); tieback-distance records not found in this pass. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/field-development/article/16763670/perdido-advances-deepwater-gom-production-possibilities)
- longest subsea tieback distance: 109 km at 1,615 m water depth; Mensa, Gulf of Mexico: 68 miles (about 109 km) in 5,300 ft of water, the world's longest subsea tieback at the time.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/home/article/16755757/shell-drills-record-tieback-well-to-counter-sand-watercut-problems)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; depth-record developments (Perdido/Tobago) are recorded in performance. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Thermal EOR (steam flood, SAGD, CSS)
<a id="ups-thermal-eor"></a>

Steam lowers heavy-oil viscosity so it flows: steam flooding (commercial in California since the 1960s), cyclic steam stimulation and SAGD, where a steam injector sits 4 to 6 m above a horizontal producer. SAGD recovery is 60 to 80% in good reservoirs with steam-oil ratios of 2.8 to 4.0 in one Clearwater case.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    STEAM["Generate steam<br/><small>once-through steam generators or boilers</small>"]
    INJ["Inject steam into the reservoir<br/><small>injection wells</small>"]
    PROD["Produce hot oil and water by gravity<br/><small>progressing cavity pumps or ESPs</small>"]
    STEAM --> INJ
    INJ --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** steam generators, horizontal well pairs, artificial lift, water treatment.

**Performance:**
- SAGD recovery factor: 60-80 % of oil in place; one section says up to 60%, another 70 to 80% in suitable reservoirs. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam-assisted_gravity_drainage)
- SAGD cumulative steam-oil ratio (Clearwater/Lower Grand Rapids): 2.8-4 t/t; 100% steam quality; operating pressure 3 to 5 MPa. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam-assisted_gravity_drainage)
- economic oil price per National Energy Board: 30-35 USD/bbl (at least); dollar year not stated. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam-assisted_gravity_drainage)
- natural gas per barrel, SAGD vs CSS: 1.08-2.14 GJ/bbl; SAGD 1.08, cyclic steam 2.14; Canadian Energy Research Institute via Wikipedia, year not stated. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_oil_sands)
- SAGD emissions intensity: 48.3-61.3 kg CO2e per bbl dilbit; OPGEE: 61.3 kg CO2e/bbl for SAGD and 48.3 for solvent-aided SAGD.. [source](https://albertainnovates.ca/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/LCA-Oil-Sands-Final-Report-December-10th-2019.pdf)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.

## Twenty-year wellhead platform development and cost history (2006–2026)
<a id="ups-whp-twenty-year-retrospective"></a>

A 20-year retrospective assembled from dated project, technology and cost evidence on Malaysian and regional wellhead platforms, recorded with sources and dollar years where published.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    BASE_2006["Baseline conventional WHP practice (2006)<br/><small>conventional platform</small>"]
    MIN_FACILITY["Minimum-facility and conductor-supported WHPs<br/><small>conductor-supported WHP, jacket WHP</small>"]
    UNMANNED["Unmanned operation and remote monitoring<br/><small>SCADA, F&G, solar-battery</small>"]
    TODAY["Current tieback and digital practice (2026)<br/><small>subsea tieback, MPFM</small>"]
    BASE_2006 --> MIN_FACILITY
    MIN_FACILITY --> UNMANNED
    UNMANNED --> TODAY
```

**Key equipment:** wellhead platforms, conductor-supported WHPs, subsea tiebacks, multiphase meters.

**Performance:**
- Operational history — Golok discovered (gas in multiple reservoirs): no range recorded; OGJ reported that Belum, Golok, Maharani and Serampang found gas in multiple reservoirs in 2005; Murphy's 2005 10-K lists Golok among discoveries added in SK309/SK311.. [source](https://www.ogj.com/general-interest/companies/article/17224324/a-malaysian-perspective)
- Operational history — First gas from SK309/SK311 Sarawak Gas Project: no range recorded; Production commenced 17 September 2009 from natural gas fields in Blocks SK309 and SK311, supplied to the PETRONAS LNG Complex at Bintulu; Murphy 85%, PETRONAS Carigali 15%.. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/news-releases/news-release-details/murphy-oil-announces-first-production-sarawak-natural-gas/)
- Operational history — Serampang gas discovery: no range recorded; OGJ reported Belum, Golok, Maharani and Serampang found gas in multiple reservoirs in 2005; Murphy's 2005 10-K also lists Serampang among discoveries added.. [source](https://www.ogj.com/general-interest/companies/article/17224324/a-malaysian-perspective)
- Operational history — Gas production start for SK309/SK311 Sarawak Gas Project: no range recorded; Serampang is part of the multi-phase development whose first production started 17 September 2009, with gas supplied to the PETRONAS LNG Complex at Bintulu.. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/news-releases/news-release-details/murphy-oil-announces-first-production-sarawak-natural-gas/)
- Operational history — PETRONAS decided not to award the Tembikai & Chenang small field RSC after competitive bidding: no range recorded; PETRONAS media release dated 25 January 2013 stated it would not proceed with the award and would not re-open the cluster for bidding.. [source](https://www.petronas.com/media/media-releases/petronas-clarifies-status-tembikai-and-chenang-rsc)
- Operational history — First oil from Tembikai field: no range recorded; Offshore Technology states oil production from the Tembikai oil field began in October 2015 from a separate development with a central processing platform and FSO; PETRONAS later stated first oil in June 2015.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/tembikai-non-associated-gas-development/)
- Operational history — First ZEEPod installed and commissioned at TNAG: no range recorded; ZEE Engineering states the first ZEEPod was installed and commissioned in July 2019 for Vestigo Petroleum; MMHE/MHB delivered topside and substructure from its Pasir Gudang/West yard in a nine-month schedule.. [source](https://zee-eng.com/blog-detail/zeepod-is-reshaping-the-future-of-marginal-oil-amp-gas-field-development-in-malaysia-the-future-of-marginal-oil-amp-gas-field-development-in-malaysia)
- Operational history — Tembikai gas pipelay completed: no range recorded; OGJ reported the 68-km 12-in subsea pipelay to the Berantai FPSO completed in July 2020; gas production was expected to commence in 2020.. [source](https://www.ogj.com/pipelines-transportation/pipelines/article/14180036/petronas-completes-tembikai-gas-pipelay)
- Reliability — ZEEPod TNAG units in operation: 5 years of operation; ZEE Engineering delivered-units table lists ZEEPod No. 1 TNAG - VESTIGO, water depth 70 m, topside lifting weight 400 MT, status '5 Years Operation' (page undated, accessed 2026). [source](https://zee-eng.com/zeepod)
- Operational history — West Patricia first oil: no range recorded; Murphy Sarawak Oil started first oil production from West Patricia in Block SK309 in May 2003, 40 km offshore Bintulu in 130 ft of water.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/production/article/16771129/murphy-oil-has-first-production-in-malaysia)
- Operational history — West Patricia and Congkak combined production: no range recorded; Murphy's 2005 10-K reported about 13,500 net barrels of oil per day from West Patricia and Congkak, with 2006 output expected to decline 10-15% on PSC entitlement effects.. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000119312506054960/d10k.htm)
- Operational history — Second West Patricia wellhead platform work started: no range recorded; Upstream reported in December 2011 that Murphy had started work on a new wellhead platform to boost capacity at West Patricia to handle more output from SK309/SK311.. [source](https://www.upstreamonline.com/weekly/murphy-double-for-malaysia/1-1-977294)
- Operational history — Water injection / capacity support works at West Patricia: no range recorded; Vantris Energy (formerly SapuraKencana) track record lists EPCIC of Patricia Satellite PT-SA and West Patricia WP-PA topside facilities for SK309/311, February to August 2013.. [source](https://vantrisenergy.com/operations-maintenance/hook-up-and-commissioning/track-record)
- Operational history — FSO swap at West Patricia: no range recorded; Malaysia Marine Department notice 96/2026(T) advised that NEPTUNE MOON would arrive on 14 July 2026 to temporarily replace FSO CASPIAN SEA until 18 September 2026, while CASPIAN SEA sailed to Singapore for maintenance on 19 July 2026.. [source](https://www.marine.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/96-2026T-%E2%80%93-NOTICE-OF-TEMPORARY-REPLACEMENT-OF-FSO-CASPIAN-SEA-BY-NEPTUNE-MOON-AT-WEST-PATRICIA-FIELD-BLOCK-SK309-OFFSHORE-SARAWAK-WITHIN-THE-EXCLUSIVE-ECONOMIC-ZONE.pdf)
- Reliability — share of SK309/SK311 oil production attributable to West Patricia: 75 percent approximately; About 7,400 barrels of oil per day produced in 2012 in Blocks SK309/311, almost 75% at West Patricia (Murphy 2012 Form 10-K). [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000119312513082919/d446290d10k.htm)
- Operational history — HOA for gas sales signed: no range recorded; A Barrel Full chronology records a heads of agreement for gas sales signed in 2006, followed by FEED completion for phase 1 in 2007 and first gas in 2009.. [source](https://www.abarrelfull.co.uk/Sarawak_Gas_Project)
- Operational history — East Patricia gas discovery: no range recorded; Murphy announced the East Patricia discovery in SK309 on 12 June 2009 with about 230 ft of net gas pay in 89 ft of water, 23 miles from the Bintulu gas receiving facility; Murphy 60%, PETRONAS Carigali 40%.. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/news-releases/news-release-details/murphy-oil-announces-additional-discoveries-malaysia)
- Operational history — Gas sales agreement extended to September 2021: no range recorded; Murphy exercised its option in December 2012 to extend the PETRONAS gas sales agreement, lengthening the contract from 2014 to September 2021.. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000119312513082919/d446290d10k.htm)
- Operational history — Five new SK309/SK311 fields on production: no range recorded; Serendah, Patricia, South Acis, Permas and Merapuh were brought on production in the second half of 2013 through platforms and pipelines tied back to existing infrastructure.. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000071742315000016/mur-20141231x10k.htm)
- Operational history — Murphy sold 30% of its Malaysian interests: no range recorded; Murphy completed sale of 20% of its Malaysian oil and gas interests in December 2014 and an additional 10% in January 2015, reducing its SK309/SK311 interest to 59.5%.. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000071742315000016/mur-20141231x10k.htm)
- Operational history — PTTEP acquired Murphy's Malaysia business: no range recorded; PTTEP HK Offshore signed an SSPA on 21 March 2019 to acquire 100% of Murphy's Malaysian business for USD 2,127 million, including the SK309 & SK311 project; completion expected end Q2 2019.. [source](https://www.pttep.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/460/pttep-acquires-murphy-oil-corporation-s-business-in-malaysia-strengthen-long-term-growth-in-southeast-asia)
- Operational history — D28 Phase 1 conductor-supported platform installed offshore Sarawak: no range recorded; 2H Offshore completed concept, detailed design, transportation and installation in 35 m water for OceanMight on behalf of PETRONAS Carigali; jack-up rig installation used for both jacket and topside.. [source](https://2hoffshore.com/about/news/2h-offshore-installs-its-eighth-conductor-supported-platform-offshore-malaysia)
- Operational history — D18 Phase 2 conductor-supported platform installed offshore Sarawak: no range recorded; 2H's eighth conductor-supported platform in Malaysian waters; installed in 35 m water, jacket and topside in the same campaign and handed over to drilling and completions.. [source](https://2hoffshore.com/about/news/2h-offshore-installs-its-eighth-conductor-supported-platform-offshore-malaysia)
- Operational history — Kikeh first production: no range recorded; Kikeh, Malaysia's first deepwater development, had been in production since 2007 (Murphy operations summary, 2013).. [source](http://supplybase.blogspot.com/2013/11/murphy-oil-malaysia.html)
- Operational history — East Patricia and Siakap North discoveries announced: no range recorded; Murphy announced East Patricia in SK309 (about 230 ft net gas pay, 89 ft water) and Siakap North in Block K (about 4,300 ft water, 80% Murphy) on 12 June 2009.. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/news-releases/news-release-details/murphy-oil-announces-additional-discoveries-malaysia)
- Operational history — Kakap-Gumusut full-field production startup: no range recorded; Murphy's 2014 10-K states the Kakap main production facility was completed and full-field production started in October 2014, with early production via a temporary tie-back to Kikeh from late 2012.. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000071742315000016/mur-20141231x10k.htm)

[source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/business-briefs/company-news/article/16782486/2h-offshore-to-deliver-lightweight-conductor-supported-platform)
- Operational history — D28 Phase 1 WHP installed: no range recorded; March 2019 report of completed jackup installation offshore Sarawak.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/field-development/article/16790310/2h-offshore-jackup-sets-down-lightweight-platform-offshore-sarawak)
- Operational history — D18 Phase 2 WHP installed: no range recorded; May 2020 report of second platform of its kind installed by jackup for PETRONAS in Malaysia.. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/478690-2h-offshore-installs-wellhead-platform-offshore-malaysia)
- Reliability — Install method: no range recorded; Offshore Magazine 2019: both substructure and topsides installed using a jackup drilling rig, a first for PETRONAS offshore Malaysia.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/field-development/article/16790310/2h-offshore-jackup-sets-down-lightweight-platform-offshore-sarawak)
- Operational history — WSS ordered: no range recorded; Petrofac and PETRONAS ordered the unmanned minimal facilities platform; first Sea Swift-type installation in Asia/Pacific; production planned for 2012.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/16760031/conductor-supported-platform-brings-multiple-benefits-for-malaysia-field-tie-in)
- Reliability — Fatigue-life sensitivity to site shift: no range recorded; Offshore Magazine 2012: moving platform a few kilometres could lessen fatigue life from 15 to three years, unacceptable; structure redesigned for 10-year minimum.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/16760031/conductor-supported-platform-brings-multiple-benefits-for-malaysia-field-tie-in)
- Operational history — FID taken: no range recorded; Offshore Magazine 2022: Sarawak Shell and partners took FID in August 2021; PETRONAS 2023 confirms two years from FID to first gas.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/regional-reports/asia/article/14284091/timi-field-platform-completed-at-sarawak-yard)
- Operational history — Mechanical completion: no range recorded; Brooke Dockyard completed fixed offshore structure works at Demak yard; event 1 Oct 2022.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/regional-reports/asia/article/14284091/timi-field-platform-completed-at-sarawak-yard)
- Operational history — First gas production: no range recorded; PETRONAS: first gas on 22 August 2023.. [source](https://www.petronas.com/media/media-releases/first-gas-production-sk318-timi-field)
- Reliability — Power system: no range recorded; PETRONAS 2023: designed unmanned and powered by solar and wind hybrid power energy system; less than 5 kW continuous generation.. [source](https://www.petronas.com/media/media-releases/first-gas-production-sk318-timi-field)
- Operational history — Field discovered: no range recorded; Offshore Technology: discovered in the 1970s; about 150 vertically stacked Miocene sand reservoirs.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/bokor-phase-3-redevelopment-project/)
- Operational history — First oil: no range recorded; Offshore Technology project profile.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/bokor-phase-3-redevelopment-project/)

- Operational history — Phase 3 construction: no range recorded; Offshore Technology: construction began 2018, completion expected 2020; WHP FEED/EPCIC awarded to Brooke Dockyard April 2017.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/bokor-phase-3-redevelopment-project/)
- Reliability — First oil year: no range recorded; Offshore Technology: Bokor produced first oil in 1982; artificial lift reviewed in 2007 after decline.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/bokor-phase-3-redevelopment-project/)

- Operational history — FID for first operated Malaysian project: no range recorded; The Star: PTTEP made FID in February 2026.. [source](https://www.thestar.com.my/business/business-news/2026/06/01/brooke-holdings-wins-rm1bil-oil-and-gas-job)

- Operational history — First commercial gas: no range recorded; MSOSH: first gas flowed December 1984; AWT case study: first commercial gas April 1984 — two dated sources with different months, both recorded.. [source](https://award39.msosh.org.my/petronas-duyong-platform)

- Operational history — Tukau discovery: no range recorded; OSTI: discovered by TK-2 in 1966 with 235 ft net oil sand and 16 ft wet gas sand.. [source](https://www.osti.gov/biblio/6859707)

- Price — pipeline EPCI and pre-commissioning contract value: 59 USD million (2019$); Alam Maritim Resources Berhad contract for engineering, procurement, construction, installation and pre-commissioning of the Tembikai gas pipeline system, awarded April 2019; dollar year not stated. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/projects/tembikai-non-associated-gas-development/)
- Price — EPC substructures and templates for Patricia and Serendah platforms: 33.4 USD million (2012$); Kencana Petroleum contract from Murphy Sarawak Oil, reported approximately RM101 million (USD 33.4 million), delivered 1st-3rd quarter 2012; dollar year not stated. [source](https://www.offshore-energy.biz/2012/02/23/kencana-lands-epc-deal-for-project-offshore-malaysia)
- Price — acquisition price for 100% of Murphy Oil's Malaysia business: 2127 USD million (2019$); PTTEP press release 21 March 2019: acquisition worth USD 2,127 million covering five projects including SK309 & SK311; dollar year not stated. [source](https://www.pttep.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/460/pttep-acquires-murphy-oil-corporation-s-business-in-malaysia-strengthen-long-term-growth-in-southeast-asia)
- Price — finding and development cost, company-wide: 11 USD per barrel of oil equivalent (less than) (2018$); Murphy 2018 annual report: 166% organic reserve replacement with finding and development costs below $11/BOE; dollar year 2018. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/static-files/612fc5f0-5d77-4441-a7c0-5dfffc905e63)
- Price — realised oil price, company-wide: 64 USD per barrel (2018$); Murphy 2018 annual report: $64/bbl realised price in 2018; dollar year 2018. [source](https://ir.murphyoilcorp.com/static-files/612fc5f0-5d77-4441-a7c0-5dfffc905e63)
- Price — write-off of prior-year Kenarong and Pertang well costs: 47.4 USD million (2014$); Murphy 2014 Form 10-K: prior-year well costs of $47.4 million related to Kenarong and Pertang written off in 2014 after PETRONAS rejected an extension of the gas holding agreement; dollar year not stated in filing. [source](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/717423/000071742315000016/mur-20141231x10k.htm)
- Price — Cash consideration for DIALOG acquisition of Tarpon Malaysia + TSIL assets: 1.19 USD million (as printed) (2023$); DIALOG press release dated 15 Nov 2023: cash consideration approximately USD1.19 million (approximately RM5.5 million).. ## Unmanned and normally unattended installations
<a id="ups-unmanned-installations"></a>

Small platforms, often just a well bay and helipad, operated remotely and visited for maintenance; an emergency shelter gives refuge if weather prevents crew return. They cost less than full platforms in shallow water, with examples in the Southern North Sea and off Western Australia.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    REM["Remote control and monitoring from a host or shore<br/><small>control system, communications</small>"]
    VIS["Periodic visits by helicopter or boat<br/><small>helipad, shelter</small>"]
    REM --> VIS
```

**Key equipment:** well bay, helipad, emergency shelter, remote control and communications.

**Performance:**
- capex reduction for a fully unmanned platform: 30 % of capex; Equinor stated 30% lower capex and 50% lower opex than a traditional concept for the fully unmanned Oseberg H.. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/features/inside-the-first-fully-automated-offshore-platform)

**Missing inputs:**
- capex and opex savings with basis (costs): Not quantified. Web-checked 2026-10-28; NUI savings figures appear only in vendor/operator presentations, no open quantified study found. Where: Offshore Magazine remote operations report (open). Next: Add a saving with source.
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Web-checked 2026-10-28; named UK NUIs exist in trade press but no verifiable figures page found. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). ## Waterflooding and low-salinity water injection
<a id="ups-waterflood-low-salinity"></a>

Water is injected to hold pressure and sweep oil; without injection only about 30% of oil is typically recovered. Low-salinity injection swaps divalent cations bound to clay for sodium, freeing oil; BP estimated about 42 million barrels extra at Clair Ridge (640 million barrels recoverable in total).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    TREAT["Filter and treat injection water (to about 2 micrometres)<br/><small>filters, deaerator, desalination for low salinity</small>"]
    INJ["Inject at 3,000 psi or more<br/><small>injection pumps, injection wells</small>"]
    PROD["Produce oil and recycle produced water<br/><small>separators</small>"]
    TREAT --> INJ
    INJ --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** injection pumps, filtration, desalination (low-salinity), injection wells, produced water system.

**Performance:**
- oil recovery without injection: 30 % (typical); Wikipedia. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterflooding)
- Clair Ridge LoSal incremental oil: 42 million barrels; BP estimate versus seawater flooding; 640 million bbl total recoverable. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/features/featurereduced-salinity-water-injection-losal-eor-clair-ridge/)
- Buzzard injection: 250000 bbl water/day (up to); to up to 11 wells. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterflooding)
- BP LoSal low-salinity waterflood expected uplift at Clair Ridge: 40 million barrels additional oil (expected); "BP said the use of its low salinity water injection EOR technology would deliver an additional 40m barrels from Clair Ridge" (first oil 23 Nov 2018). Actual production results and the injected-water salinity figure were not stated in open pages read. [source](https://www.energyvoice.com/oilandgas/north-sea/186750/major-milestone-as-bp-hits-clair-ridge-first-oil)

**Costs:**
- desalination facilities for Clair Ridge: 120 USD million (earmarked); dollar year not stated; development cost GBP 4.5 billion. [source](https://www.offshore-technology.com/features/featurereduced-salinity-water-injection-losal-eor-clair-ridge/)

**Reference plants:**
- Clair Ridge low-salinity waterflood project, west of Shetland, GB: USD 4.5 bn development targeting 640 million boe over about 40 years; world-first commercial low-salinity (LoSal) water injection EOR; first oil 23 Nov 2018; operating. [source](https://www.energyvoice.com/oilandgas/north-sea/186750/major-milestone-as-bp-hits-clair-ridge-first-oil)

**Missing inputs:**
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. IEA ETP Guide (July 2026) has no matching entry (searched waterflood, low salinity, water injection, flooding); 2026-10-28 no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterflooding), [2](https://www.offshore-technology.com/features/featurereduced-salinity-water-injection-losal-eor-clair-ridge/), [3](https://www.energyvoice.com/oilandgas/north-sea/186750/major-milestone-as-bp-hits-clair-ridge-first-oil), [4](https://eniec.cug.edu.cn/__local/2/A5/CC/25BA71EF43CDE17B78ADB0759AD_8F3908F6_F86B72.pdf). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Well plug and abandonment (cement plugs, rig-based)
<a id="ups-well-plug-abandonment"></a>

A well that is no longer needed is permanently sealed so that formation fluids cannot reach the surface. Barriers, usually cement plugs set across producing zones, above the liner top and near the surface, are placed from a rig after the well is killed and equipment is removed. Standards differ in the barriers they require, for example Indonesia's SNI 13-6910-2002 and Norway's NORSOK D-010, and the choice drives cost. Newer methods such as through-tubing abandonment aim to avoid pulling the completion.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    KIL["Well kill and completion removal<br/><small>rig or intervention unit, kill fluids, workover tools</small>"]
    BAR["Barrier placement<br/><small>cement plugs across perforations, liner top and surface, cement unit and additives</small>"]
    VER["Barrier verification<br/><small>pressure and tag tests</small>"]
    CUT["Wellhead and casing cutting<br/><small>casing cutters, wellhead removal</small>"]
    SIT["Site restoration"]
    KIL --> BAR
    BAR --> VER
    VER --> CUT
    CUT --> SIT
```

**Key equipment:** workover or drilling rig, cement unit, casing cutting tools, pressure testing equipment.

**Performance:**
- cement plugs in the SNI design for directional well H-11, Tambun field: 7 plugs; total 160.11 bbl cement (576 sacks), deepest plug 8,300-8,638 ft MD; one well, design study. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2141)
- UKCS well abandonment spend in 2025: 1273 GBP million (in-year prices); 49% of total decommissioning spend; 2024 was GBP 1,168 million. [source](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/w1fa3xvv/ukcs-decommissioning-cost-and-performance-update-2026.pdf)
- suspended UKCS wells awaiting final abandonment: 500 wells (approximate); backlog unchanged from 2024. [source](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/w1fa3xvv/ukcs-decommissioning-cost-and-performance-update-2026.pdf)

**Costs:**
- estimated cost of abandoning directional well H-11 by SNI 13-6910-2002: 179276 USD (2026$); rig method, onshore Tambun field, Indonesia; design estimate, calculated in 2026 using the SNI 13-6910-2002 method ("Based on calculations in 2026 ... the estimated cost for the H-11 well is USD 179,276.30"); the paper separately projects USD 505,737.59 for 2066. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2141)
- estimated cost of abandoning the same well by NORSOK D-010: 414945 USD (2026$); rig method, onshore Tambun field, Indonesia; design estimate, calculated in 2026 using the NORSOK D-010 method ("using the NORSOK Standard D-010 method, the cost is USD 414,945.09"); the paper separately projects USD 1,170,558.11 for 2066. [source](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2141)
- average UKCS well decommissioning spend per well worked on, 2025 (derived): 4.95 GBP million; derived from stated 2025 actuals: GBP 1,273 million spent on decommissioning work on 257 wells, of which 114 reached fully decommissioned AB3 status; in-year (2025) prices; average computed from the stated spend and well count. [source](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/w1fa3xvv/ukcs-decommissioning-cost-and-performance-update-2026.pdf)
- operator-reported saving per well on a recent eight-well UKCS campaign: 250000 GBP; saving of more than GBP 250,000 per well, approximately GBP 2 million in total on eight wells, attributed to using one supplier for multiple services and a small operational offshore footprint; currency year not stated, publication year used. [source](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/w1fa3xvv/ukcs-decommissioning-cost-and-performance-update-2026.pdf)

**Missing inputs:**
- average P&A cost per well offshore Southeast Asia and per well in the UKCS (costs): The NSTA report read gives totals and spend, not a clear cost per well; the LEMIGAS figure is one onshore well. UKCS per-well spend and an operator per-well saving have been added from the NSTA 2026 update; a Southeast Asia per-well average is still not in the sources read. Searched LEMIGAS SCOG corpus for per well, average, offshore, benchmark; the H-11 paper is a single onshore well and names no Southeast Asia offshore average; note its USD 505,737.59 / 1,170,558.11 figures are projected 2066 values, the 2026 calculations are USD 179,276.30 / 414,945.09; 2026-10-28 Where: NSTA decommissioning benchmarking report (appendices); SKK Migas decommissioning guidance (catalog: uk-nsta, open). Next: Add cost per well with dollar year and basis from the NSTA appendices or SKK Migas.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA guide covers energy-transition technologies, not upstream oil and gas process units; no agency (IEA, IRENA, DOE, DEA) publishes a TRL for this equipment class. Checked 2026-11-16. Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (already searched, 634 rows) (open); Sector regulator or industry body publishing a TRL for this class (open). Next: Add the TRL only when an agency publishes one for this technology; do not infer it from 'commercial' maturity.

Sources: [1](https://journal.lemigas.esdm.go.id/index.php/SCOG/article/view/2141), [2](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/w1fa3xvv/ukcs-decommissioning-cost-and-performance-update-2026.pdf), [3](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/3rmh154v/the-wells-decommissioning-collab-overview.pdf), [4](https://www.ibp.org.br/media/diretrizes-para-abandono-de-pocos-ingles.pdf). ## Wellhead platform control, safety and communication systems
<a id="ups-whp-control-safety-and-communication-systems"></a>

Wellhead platform control, safety and communication systems: SCADA/DCS, ESD/SDV, fire and gas, navigation aids, telecom and condition monitoring. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PROCESS_CONTROL_AN["Process control and monitoring via SCADA/DCS, remote from shore or hub platform<br/><small>Process control and monitoring via SCADA/DCS, remote from shore or hub platform</small>"]
    ESD["ESD<br/><small>ESD</small>"]
    FIRE_AND_GAS["Fire and Gas<br/><small>Fire and Gas</small>"]
    NAVIGATION_AIDS["Navigation aids<br/><small>Navigation aids</small>"]
    PLATFORM_TELECOM_V["Platform telecom: VHF/UHF radio, satellite communication<br/><small>Platform telecom: VHF/UHF radio, satellite communication</small>"]
    CONDITION_MONITORI["Condition monitoring instrumentation<br/><small>Condition monitoring instrumentation</small>"]
    PROCESS_CONTROL_AN --> ESD
    ESD --> FIRE_AND_GAS
    FIRE_AND_GAS --> NAVIGATION_AIDS
    NAVIGATION_AIDS --> PLATFORM_TELECOM_V
    PLATFORM_TELECOM_V --> CONDITION_MONITORI
```

**Key equipment:** Process control and monitoring via SCADA/DCS, remote from shore or hub platform, ESD, Fire and Gas, Navigation aids, Platform telecom: VHF/UHF radio, satellite communication, Condition monitoring instrumentation.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — SCADA failover architecture: no range recorded; primary and backup station per node, configurable failover timeout; control remains in PLC/RTU if SCADA is down. [source](https://tatsoft.com/oil-gas/)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.masibus.com/projects-executed/scada-projects-executed/wellhead-monitoring-and-control-system-for-offshore-platform)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.tdl-ia.com/offshore-platform-fso-automation.html)
- Reliability — Required SIL - ESD isolation of production/injection bore: 3 SIL; Offshore Norge guideline No. 070 rev 06, Table A.6.5. [source](https://www.offshorenorge.no/contentassets/adc7e1512f90400cb7fe9f314600bed6/070---recommended-guidelines-for-the-application-of-iec-61508-and-iec-61511-rev.-06.pdf)
- Reliability — Required SIL - ESD isolation of annulus in one topside gas lift well: 3 SIL; Offshore Norge guideline No. 070 rev 06, Table A.6.5. [source](https://www.offshorenorge.no/contentassets/adc7e1512f90400cb7fe9f314600bed6/070---recommended-guidelines-for-the-application-of-iec-61508-and-iec-61511-rev.-06.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.offshorenorge.no/contentassets/adc7e1512f90400cb7fe9f314600bed6/070---recommended-guidelines-for-the-application-of-iec-61508-and-iec-61511-rev.-06.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-30/section-250.855)
- Reliability — PFDavg: 0.00029 dimensionless; Consilium CS-PH Ex, MTTR 8 h, 1-year proof test interval. [source](https://www.consiliumsafety.com/content/uploads/products/en/5210021-00/5210021-00A_CS-PH%20Ex_M_EN_2024_X.pdf)
- Reliability — F&G logic PFD (single I/O, redundant CPU): 0.0012 dimensionless; Offshore Norge Table A.8.1, voting 1oo1. [source](https://www.offshorenorge.no/contentassets/adc7e1512f90400cb7fe9f314600bed6/070---recommended-guidelines-for-the-application-of-iec-61508-and-iec-61511-rev.-06.pdf)
- Reliability — Required SIL - fire detection with one detector: 2 SIL; Offshore Norge section A.7: fire detector (heat, flame or smoke) + F&G logic incl I/O. [source](https://www.offshorenorge.no/contentassets/adc7e1512f90400cb7fe9f314600bed6/070---recommended-guidelines-for-the-application-of-iec-61508-and-iec-61511-rev.-06.pdf)
- Reliability — Required SIL - gas detection with one detector: 2 SIL; Offshore Norge section A.8.1: catalytic, IR point, IR line or H2S detector + F&G logic incl I/O. **Costs:**
- Price — Wellhead monitoring installed hardware per well (onshore proxy, NOT offshore WHP): 3000-8000 USD (2026$); US onshore Permian wellhead monitoring hardware: two pressure transmitters, temperature sensor, cellular gateway, panel, solar/battery, install labour; vendor guide dated 6 May 2026. [source](https://www.merobix.com/blog/wellhead-monitoring-system-guide.html)
- Price — Upfront implementation, traditional on-premise SCADA for 10 wells (onshore proxy): 80000-200000 USD (2026$); vendor guide dated 6 May 2026; onshore US, not an offshore platform quote. [source](https://www.merobix.com/blog/wellhead-monitoring-system-guide.html)
- Price — 4-20 mA wellhead pressure transmitter (onshore proxy): 300-800 USD (2026$); Quality oilfield pressure transmitters from major industrial instrumentation manufacturers; US onshore guide dated 6 May 2026. [source](https://www.merobix.com/blog/wellhead-monitoring-system-guide.html)

## Wellhead platform production process systems
<a id="ups-whp-production-process-systems"></a>

Wellhead platform production process systems: manifolds, chokes, pig traps, chemical injection, drains, vents and sampling. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PRODUCTION_MANIFOL["Production manifold / headers with production and test headers to commingle flui<br/><small>Production manifold / headers with production and test headers to commingle flui</small>"]
    CHOKES_AND_CHOKE_M["Chokes and choke manifolds<br/><small>Chokes and choke manifolds</small>"]
    PIG_LAUNCHER_RECEI["Pig launcher / receiver for the export pipeline<br/><small>Pig launcher / receiver for the export pipeline</small>"]
    EXPORT_RISERS_AND["Export risers and subsea pipeline tie-ins to the CPP or FSO<br/><small>Export risers and subsea pipeline tie-ins to the CPP or FSO</small>"]
    CHEMICAL_INJECTION["Chemical injection system<br/><small>Chemical injection system</small>"]
    CLOSED_DRAIN_SYSTE["Closed drain system and open/slop drain with sump caisson<br/><small>Closed drain system and open/slop drain with sump caisson</small>"]
    VENTS_FLARE_TIP["Vents / flare tip<br/><small>Vents / flare tip</small>"]
    SAMPLING_CONNECTIO["Sampling connections and gauging<br/><small>Sampling connections and gauging</small>"]
    PRODUCTION_MANIFOL --> CHOKES_AND_CHOKE_M
    CHOKES_AND_CHOKE_M --> PIG_LAUNCHER_RECEI
    PIG_LAUNCHER_RECEI --> EXPORT_RISERS_AND
    EXPORT_RISERS_AND --> CHEMICAL_INJECTION
    CHEMICAL_INJECTION --> CLOSED_DRAIN_SYSTE
    CLOSED_DRAIN_SYSTE --> VENTS_FLARE_TIP
    VENTS_FLARE_TIP --> SAMPLING_CONNECTIO
```

**Key equipment:** Production manifold / headers with production and test headers to commingle fluids, Chokes and choke manifolds, Pig launcher / receiver for the export pipeline, Export risers and subsea pipeline tie-ins to the CPP or FSO, Chemical injection system, Closed drain system and open/slop drain with sump caisson, Vents / flare tip, Sampling connections and gauging.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — Choke bean erosion sensitivity: no range recorded; US20120273228A1: 'The choke bean design is extremely sensitive to erosion and can be completely destroyed in only a few minutes if the well produces sand, debris, proppant or other particulates with high-speed flows through the choke' (no numeric failure rate printed). [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US20120273228A1/en)
- Reliability — Single subsea choke deterioration at high differential pressure: no range recorded; WO2008045381A2: excess pressure 'as high as 5000 to 6000 psi across the production choke, rapid deterioration or even failure of the choke is likely due to the severe operating conditions at the choke trim'. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/WO2008045381A2/en)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_tree_(oil_well))
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_tree_(oil_well))
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US4135949A/en)

- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US6981848B1/en)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.cra.energy/product/knockout-drums)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.cra.energy/product/knockout-drums)
- Reliability — MPFM calibration verification / proof-test interval: no range recorded; Emerson Roxar 2600 FAQ: 'with a typical recommendation being yearly checks and verification. Calibration is typically verified using test separator data or sampling' — no failure rate printed. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en/measurement-instrumentation/products/roxar-2600-multiphase-flow-meter)
- Reliability — MPFM maintenance drivers: no range recorded; Emerson: 'The meter has no moving parts, reducing wear and maintenance needs, and a robust design, reducing failures and the need for spare parts'. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en/measurement-instrumentation/products/roxar-2600-multiphase-flow-meter)

## Wellhead platform safety and emergency equipment
<a id="ups-whp-safety-and-emergency-equipment"></a>

Wellhead platform safety and emergency equipment: lifeboats/TEMPSC, life rafts, escape routes, fire equipment and ESD isolation. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    LIFEBOAT_TEMPSC_WI["Lifeboat / TEMPSC with davit<br/><small>Lifeboat / TEMPSC with davit</small>"]
    LIFE_RAFTS_RETRIEV["Life rafts, retrieval nets, sea rescue baskets<br/><small>Life rafts, retrieval nets, sea rescue baskets</small>"]
    ESCAPE_ROUTES_MUST["Escape routes, muster points, signs and safety lighting<br/><small>Escape routes, muster points, signs and safety lighting</small>"]
    FIRE_EXTINGUISHERS["Fire extinguishers, hydrant hoses, SCBA sets on manned platforms<br/><small>Fire extinguishers, hydrant hoses, SCBA sets on manned platforms</small>"]
    OVERBOARD_DUMP_VAL["Overboard dump valves and ESD-initiated isolation of all wells and pipelines<br/><small>Overboard dump valves and ESD-initiated isolation of all wells and pipelines</small>"]
    LIFEBOAT_TEMPSC_WI --> LIFE_RAFTS_RETRIEV
    LIFE_RAFTS_RETRIEV --> ESCAPE_ROUTES_MUST
    ESCAPE_ROUTES_MUST --> FIRE_EXTINGUISHERS
    FIRE_EXTINGUISHERS --> OVERBOARD_DUMP_VAL
```

**Key equipment:** Lifeboat / TEMPSC with davit, Life rafts, retrieval nets, sea rescue baskets, Escape routes, muster points, signs and safety lighting, Fire extinguishers, hydrant hoses, SCBA sets on manned platforms, Overboard dump valves and ESD-initiated isolation of all wells and pipelines.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — Release gear proof test load factor: 1.1 x total loaded mass; 46 CFR 109.301(i)(5): at least once every 5 years or whenever overhauled. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Launching appliance thorough examination interval: 5 years max; 46 CFR 109.301(i)(2), with dynamic test of winch brake on completion. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Weekly lifeboat engine run time: 3 min ahead and astern; 46 CFR 109.301(d)(2). [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Redundant braking for lifeboat winch: no range recorded; WO2013122479A1: secondary components 'ensure proper operation / braking in the event of failure of the primary system'; either accumulator can release both brakes. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/WO2013122479A1/en)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEMPSC)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEMPSC)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://solas-lsa-code.vercel.app/en/lsa/4.2/)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEMPSC)
- Reliability — Inflatable liferaft service interval: 12 months; 46 CFR 109.301(g)(1) for MODUs. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Hydrostatic release unit service interval: 12 months; 46 CFR 109.301(h)(1). [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Dual redundant lifting slings: no range recorded; Viking Life 1145-8: elastic compensator sling plus chain or wire sling, 'serving as a backup in case the other sling fails'. [source](https://www.viking-life.com/products/additional-lsa/transfer-descent-and-recovery/transfer-baskets/personnel-transfer-basket-safe-transfer-8-pers-1145-8)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://solas-lsa-code.vercel.app/en/lsa/4.2/)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://patents.google.com/patent/US7069874B2/en)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.viking-life.com/products/additional-lsa/transfer-descent-and-recovery/transfer-baskets/personnel-transfer-basket-safe-transfer-8-pers-1145-8)
- Reliability — Single point of failure tolerance: no range recorded; SOLAS II-2/13.2.1; failure of any single light or cut in a lighting strip must not make marking ineffective (13.3.2.5.1). [source](https://www.imorules.com/GUID-FD648BB1-3991-407E-AD5E-1263AA7F3AD4.html)
- Reliability — General alarm test frequency (MODU): 1 per week; 46 CFR 109.301(d)(3): 'The general alarm system must be tested' under weekly inspections and tests. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-109.301)
- Reliability — Monthly extinguisher inspection: 1 per month; 33 CFR 145.01(b)(2): monthly inspections required by NFPA 10 may be conducted by owner, operator, person-in-charge or designated crew member. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-33/part-145)
- Reliability — Annual extinguisher maintenance and hydrostatic test: 1 per year; 33 CFR 145.01(b)(1) and (4): annual maintenance/recharging to NFPA 10; hydrostatic pressure tests by qualified servicing facility. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-33/part-145)
- Reliability — SCBA readiness: no range recorded; 46 CFR 77.30-5(d) and 96.30-5(b) (search extract): all emergency equipment maintained operative; master and chief engineer ensure sufficient crew familiar with operation. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-46/section-77.30-5)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-33/part-145)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-33/section-149.417)
