## Wellhead platform utility systems
<a id="ups-whp-utility-systems"></a>

Wellhead platform utility systems: power, diesel, instrument air, water, firewater, seawater, HVAC, cranes and emergency power. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    POWER_GENERATION["Power generation<br/><small>Power generation</small>"]
    DIESEL_STORAGE_AND["Diesel storage and transfer<br/><small>Diesel storage and transfer</small>"]
    INSTRUMENT_UTILITY["Instrument/utility air compressors, receivers and dryers<br/><small>Instrument/utility air compressors, receivers and dryers</small>"]
    POTABLE_WATER_STOR["Potable water storage and hot water system<br/><small>Potable water storage and hot water system</small>"]
    FIREWATER_PUMPS_DE["Firewater pumps, deluge/sprinkler system, hydrants and hose reels<br/><small>Firewater pumps, deluge/sprinkler system, hydrants and hose reels</small>"]
    SEAWATER_LIFT_PUMP["Seawater lift pumps<br/><small>Seawater lift pumps</small>"]
    HVAC_FOR_EQUIPMENT["HVAC for equipment/MCC rooms<br/><small>HVAC for equipment/MCC rooms</small>"]
    CRANE["Crane<br/><small>Crane</small>"]
    DIESEL_DRIVEN_EMER["Diesel-driven emergency generator with UPS/battery banks<br/><small>Diesel-driven emergency generator with UPS/battery banks</small>"]
    POWER_GENERATION --> DIESEL_STORAGE_AND
    DIESEL_STORAGE_AND --> INSTRUMENT_UTILITY
    INSTRUMENT_UTILITY --> POTABLE_WATER_STOR
    POTABLE_WATER_STOR --> FIREWATER_PUMPS_DE
    FIREWATER_PUMPS_DE --> SEAWATER_LIFT_PUMP
    SEAWATER_LIFT_PUMP --> HVAC_FOR_EQUIPMENT
    HVAC_FOR_EQUIPMENT --> CRANE
    CRANE --> DIESEL_DRIVEN_EMER
```

**Key equipment:** Power generation, Diesel storage and transfer, Instrument/utility air compressors, receivers and dryers, Potable water storage and hot water system, Firewater pumps, deluge/sprinkler system, hydrants and hose reels, Seawater lift pumps, HVAC for equipment/MCC rooms, Crane, Diesel-driven emergency generator with UPS/battery banks.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — Availability of simple unmanned platforms (Types 2 and 3): 95-99.5 % uptime; Generally, there are good experiences with the UWHPs. Types 2 and 3 have very a high availability (uptime) - in the range of 95% - 99.5%. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — Host cable preferred over local diesel for reliability: no range recorded; Diesel engines cause downtime, and therefore cable supply from the host platform is preferred. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — Hybrid benefit for battery charging: no range recorded; Compared with the alternatives, it means reduced battery size, improved battery charging performance during the night and bad weather, and lower weight and capital and operating expenditure. [source](https://www.shell.com/what-we-do/technology-and-innovation/shell-techxplorer-digest/shell-techxplorer-digest-women-in-tech/_jcr_content/root/main/section/simple_633466041_cop/list/list_item_copy_1573356562.multi.stream/1660054984863/9c486b9f6b4f1800c0a16bfb526c51d26a2e3e7f/digest-wit-unmanned-wellhead-platform-powered-by-a-solar-wind-hybrid.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.shell.com/what-we-do/technology-and-innovation/shell-techxplorer-digest/shell-techxplorer-digest-women-in-tech/_jcr_content/root/main/section/simple_633466041_cop/list/list_item_copy_1573356562.multi.stream/1660054984863/9c486b9f6b4f1800c0a16bfb526c51d26a2e3e7f/digest-wit-unmanned-wellhead-platform-powered-by-a-solar-wind-hybrid.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.shell.com/what-we-do/technology-and-innovation/shell-techxplorer-digest/shell-techxplorer-digest-women-in-tech/_jcr_content/root/main/section/simple_633466041_cop/list/list_item_copy_1573356562.multi.stream/1660054984863/9c486b9f6b4f1800c0a16bfb526c51d26a2e3e7f/digest-wit-unmanned-wellhead-platform-powered-by-a-solar-wind-hybrid.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://oceannews.com/news/energy/shell-delivers-first-gas-from-the-timi-platform-in-malaysia)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.shell.com/what-we-do/technology-and-innovation/shell-techxplorer-digest/shell-techxplorer-digest-women-in-tech/_jcr_content/root/main/section/simple_633466041_cop/list/list_item_copy_1573356562.multi.stream/1660054984863/9c486b9f6b4f1800c0a16bfb526c51d26a2e3e7f/digest-wit-unmanned-wellhead-platform-powered-by-a-solar-wind-hybrid.pdf)
- Reliability — Water content - negligible / moderate / heavy: 100-200 mg/kg; Water content (e.g. by IP438): Negligible <100 mg/kg; Moderate 100 - 200 mg/kg; Heavy >200 mg/kg. [source](https://www.marinesafetyforum.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/01/MSF_Guidance-Delivering_Fuel_Cargoes_to_Offshore_Locations_Dec2018.pdf)
- Reliability — Total viable count of culturable bacteria/yeasts/moulds: 10000-100000 CFU/Litre; Total Viable Count of culturable bacteria, yeasts and/or moulds (e.g. by IP385 / ASTM D6974 or IP613): Negligible <10^4 CFU/Litre; Moderate 10^4 - 10^5 CFU/Litre; Heavy >10^5 CFU/Litre. [source](https://www.marinesafetyforum.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/01/MSF_Guidance-Delivering_Fuel_Cargoes_to_Offshore_Locations_Dec2018.pdf)
- Reliability — Microbial monitoring frequency: no range recorded; Take Bottom or Drain Sample every 1 – 3 Months; and routine monitoring of tank bottoms typically every one to six months, depending on risk. [source](https://www.marinesafetyforum.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/01/MSF_Guidance-Delivering_Fuel_Cargoes_to_Offshore_Locations_Dec2018.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.specoilandgas.com/equipment/ancillary-equipment/utilities)
- Reliability — Water quality testing frequency: no range recorded; quarterly microbial and chemical sampling ... and annual inspections to check for coating breakdown, rust, deposits or organic growth are required. [source](https://www.marinesafetyforum.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/MSF-Guidance-Delivering-Quality-PotWater-to-Offshore-Installations-18th-July-2103.pdf)
- Reliability — Uptime of simple unmanned platforms: 95-99.5 %; Types 2 and 3 have very a high availability (uptime) - in the range of 95% - 99.5%. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — Firewater minimisation recommendation: no range recorded; It is beneficial to reduce the F&G and fire water systems to an absolute minimum. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — NORSOK firewater pump requirement: no range recorded; the present legislation in Norway also requires that fire water pump(s) (a NORSOK requirement) are installed on platforms with helidecks. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — Subsea HSP multiphase pump run-life claim (adjacent technology): no range recorded; The multiphase variant of the HSP has over ten years successful operation as the primary means of production in subsea wells and has demonstrated run lives around three times longer than the industry average for electric submersible pumps. [source](https://www.celerosft.com/en-us/industries/oil-gas/upstream)
- Reliability — HVAC redundancy requirement: no range recorded; HVAC systems serving spaces where area classification depends on ventilation or where operational aspects require extensive ventilation availability shall have back-up capacity/ adequate standby /redundancy. [source](https://www.pdhonline.com/courses/m332/m332content.pdf)
- Reliability — Battery room ventilation on emergency power: no range recorded; The battery rooms shall have 100% duty plus standby explosion proof exhaust fans, with automatic change-over in event of failure of main fan. [source](https://www.pdhonline.com/courses/m332/m332content.pdf)
- Reliability — Electro-hydraulic crane versus diesel generator loading: no range recorded; The use of electro-hydraulic cranes is not recommended on installations with diesel generators, as the diesel engine will be sized for the major user: the crane. Therefore, for the majority of the time, the engine will run on a very low utilisation, which causes problems for diesel engines. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.sodir.no/4ae82e/globalassets/1-sodir/publikasjoner/rapporter/unmanned-wellhead-platforms.pdf)
- Reliability — Minimum online main generating sets: no range recorded; Typically at least two units must be running with at least one spare (possible exception for water injection). [source](https://r5.ieee.org/houston/wp-content/uploads/sites/32/2016/01/8-Offshore-Platform-Design-Apr-7-8.pdf)
- Reliability — Essential generator loads: no range recorded; Typical Types of Load to consider for Essential Generation supply are ... ; Loads to maintain a level of life support facilities ... (i.e. HVAC, lighting, kitchen, potable water, sewage, etc.) ... Diesel fuel transfer pumps ... Loads to “blackstart” one main generator set ... Instrument / plant air compressor ... Crane auxiliaries. [source](https://r5.ieee.org/houston/wp-content/uploads/sites/32/2016/01/8-Offshore-Platform-Design-Apr-7-8.pdf)
- Reliability — Two independent starting systems with automatic changeover: no range recorded; Diesel engines with two independent starting systems shall have an automatic changeover device. [source](http://jip33.iogp.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Specification-for-Diesel-Generator-Package-ISOIEC-Offshore-With-Justifications-S-714-1Jv2022-09.pdf)

**Costs:**
- Price — Wholesale electricity benchmark (North America): 0.06 USD/kWh (2013$); Wholesale electricity prices vary regionally ... but are typically about $0.06/kWh in North America at the time of writing (Chemical Engineering Design, 2nd ed., ©2013). [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — Diesel standby fuel infrastructure - day tank: 2000-8000 USD (2026$); a day tank (2,000–8,000) ... fuel infrastructure cost ranges, 2026 vendor pricing guide. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Diesel standby fuel infrastructure - bulk storage tank: 8000-60000 USD (2026$); bulk storage tank (8,000–60,000+ depending on capacity). [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Fuel infrastructure for 500 kW / 72 h autonomy: 25000-50000 USD (2026$); For a 500 kW facility requiring 72 hours of autonomous runtime, fuel infrastructure can add 25,000–50,000 to the total project. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Diesel fuel cost: 0.08-0.15 USD/kWh (2026$); Fuel cost per kWh - Diesel Standby: 0.08–0.15. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Cost of drying air for instrument air: 0.005 USD per standard m3 (2013$); Drying the air, for example for instrument air, typically adds about $0.005 per standard m 3 ($0.14/1000 scf) - Chemical Engineering Design 2nd ed., ©2013, no separate dollar year stated. [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — Raw water rough cost estimate: 2 USD per 1000 gal (2013$); A very rough estimate of water costs can be made by assuming $2 per 1000 gal ($0.5 per metric ton) - Chemical Engineering Design 2nd ed., ©2013, dollar year not separately stated. [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — Demineralised water cost multiple: 2 x raw water price; Demineralized water typically costs about double the price of raw water. [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — Cooling-water system make-up and chemical treatment cost: 0.02 USD/1000 gal (2013$); The costs of water make-up and chemical treatment usually add about $0.02/1000 gal - Chemical Engineering Design 2nd ed., ©2013. [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — Recirculating cooling water pumping power: 1-2 kWh/1000 gal circulated; The power used in a typical recirculating cooling water system is usually between 1 and 2 kWh/1000 gal of circulating water. [source](https://booksite.elsevier.com/samplechapters/9780080966595/Chapter_3.pdf)
- Price — 500 kW diesel standby genset total installed (factory-direct): 135000-305000 USD (2026$); A 500 kW commercial standby generator set costs 135,000 to 305,000 installed, with factory-direct options typically 30-50% below Tier 1 dealer quotes. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — 500 kW diesel standby genset total installed (Tier 1 dealer): 320000-600000 USD (2026$); 500 kW ... Total Installed (Tier 1): 320,000-600,000. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — 100 kW diesel standby genset total installed (factory-direct): 28000-73000 USD (2026$); 100 kW system runs 28,000-73,000 installed (factory-direct). [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — 1,000 kW diesel standby genset total installed (factory-direct): 300000-620000 USD (2026$); 1,000 kW ... Total Installed (Factory-Direct): 300,000-620,000. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Diesel genset equipment cost: 180-400 USD/kW (2026$); Equipment costs for diesel standby gensets typically fall in the 180-400 per kW range for factory-direct units. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — US large industrial total installed cost: 250-450 USD/kW (2026$); United States - Total installed cost: 300-600/kW (small commercial), 250-450/kW (large industrial). [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — Southeast Asia total installed cost: 280-550 USD/kW (2026$); Southeast Asia (Indonesia, Philippines, Vietnam) - Total installed cost: 280-550/kW. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)
- Price — ATS 500-1,000 kW total cost: 23000-60000 USD (2026$); 500-1,000 kW ATS Equipment 15,000-40,000; ATS Installation 8,000-20,000; Total ATS Cost 23,000-60,000. [source](https://zccpower.com/commercial-standby-generator-cost)

## Wellhead platform well systems
<a id="ups-whp-well-systems"></a>

Wellhead platform well systems: well systems: wellheads and trees, WCP/HPU, gas lift, water injection and well testing. Sourced from pages read in the 2026-11-24 WHP component sweep.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRILLING_SLOTS_WEL["Drilling slots / well bays<br/><small>Drilling slots / well bays</small>"]
    WELLHEADS_AND_CHRI["Wellheads and Christmas trees<br/><small>Wellheads and Christmas trees</small>"]
    WELLHEAD_CONTROL_P["Wellhead Control Panel<br/><small>Wellhead Control Panel</small>"]
    ESD_STATIONS_AT_ST["ESD stations at strategic locations<br/><small>ESD stations at strategic locations</small>"]
    GAS_LIFT_MANIFOLD["Gas lift manifold and flowlines for artificial lift<br/><small>Gas lift manifold and flowlines for artificial lift</small>"]
    WATER_INJECTION_MA["Water injection manifold and W.I. pig launcher/receiver<br/><small>Water injection manifold and W.I. pig launcher/receiver</small>"]
    WELL_TEST_FACILITI["Well test facilities: test manifold with either a test separator or a multiphase<br/><small>Well test facilities: test manifold with either a test separator or a multiphase</small>"]
    DRILLING_SLOTS_WEL --> WELLHEADS_AND_CHRI
    WELLHEADS_AND_CHRI --> WELLHEAD_CONTROL_P
    WELLHEAD_CONTROL_P --> ESD_STATIONS_AT_ST
    ESD_STATIONS_AT_ST --> GAS_LIFT_MANIFOLD
    GAS_LIFT_MANIFOLD --> WATER_INJECTION_MA
    WATER_INJECTION_MA --> WELL_TEST_FACILITI
```

**Key equipment:** Drilling slots / well bays, Wellheads and Christmas trees, Wellhead Control Panel, ESD stations at strategic locations, Gas lift manifold and flowlines for artificial lift, Water injection manifold and W.I. pig launcher/receiver, Well test facilities: test manifold with either a test separator or a multiphase flow meter.

**Performance:**
- Reliability — operational design life: 20 years; Tarpon platforms designed for a 20-year operational lifespan and relocatable. [source](https://www.tarponsystems.com/installation-of-two-wellhead-platforms-for-kayu-manis-south-east-and-anjung-kecil)
- Reliability — minimum design fatigue life: 10 years; West Desaru WSS minimum 10-year design fatigue life; structure re-designed after slot growth. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/16760031/conductor-supported-platform-brings-multiple-benefits-for-malaysia-field-tie-in)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/16760031/conductor-supported-platform-brings-multiple-benefits-for-malaysia-field-tie-in)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.tarponsystems.com/installation-of-two-wellhead-platforms-for-kayu-manis-south-east-and-anjung-kecil)
- Reliability — two master valves used for redundancy: 2 valves; DrillingFormulas: two master valves provide redundancy so another can perform the function if one fails. [source](https://www.drillingformulas.com/surface-christmas-tree-dry-tree-basic-knowlege/)
- Reliability — fail-safe closed hydraulic wing valve: 0 count; Hydraulic operated wing valves usually fail safe closed, requiring active hydraulic pressure to stay open; loss of control fluid shuts the well in. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_tree_(oil_well))
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_tree_(oil_well))
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.bakerhughes.com/subsea/subsea-production-systems/well-system-solutions)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.technipfmc.com/en/media/news/2025/05/technipfmc-marks-milestone-100th-subsea-20-tree-delivery-with-client-that-placed-first-order/)
- Reliability — accumulator precharge check interval: 1 year; The pre-charge pressure must be checked at least once per year and if necessary charged again. [source](https://www.ipsgroupbv.com/products/turnkey-control-systems/wellhead-control-panel-multi/)
- Reliability — pump redundancy: 2 pumps per pressure level; Each pressure level has two dedicated electric driven pumps (A and B). [source](https://www.ipsgroupbv.com/products/turnkey-control-systems/wellhead-control-panel-multi/)
- Reliability — design operational life: 25 years; INTECH panels can be designed for up to 25 years of operational life, with optional extended O&M support. [source](https://www.intechww.com/skids-packages/wellhead-solutions/wellhead-control-panels)
- Reliability — hydraulic system low-pressure trip: 0 count; API RP 14C training slides list 'Low pressure hydraulic system' as an input to ESD II. [source](https://pdfcoffee.com/safety-design-and-api-rp-14c-2-pdf-free.html)
- Reliability — SIL 1 probability of dangerous failure on demand (low demand): 0.01-0.1 PFD; PFD 0.1-0.01, RRF 10-100, low demand operation per IEC EN 61508 as printed in table. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_Integrity_Level)
- Reliability — SIL 2 probability of dangerous failure on demand (low demand): 0.001-0.01 PFD; PFD 0.01-0.001, RRF 100-1000, low demand operation per IEC EN 61508. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_Integrity_Level)
- Reliability — SIL 3 probability of dangerous failure on demand (low demand): 0.0001-0.001 PFD; PFD 0.001-0.0001, RRF 1000-10,000, low demand operation per IEC EN 61508. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_Integrity_Level)
- Reliability — SIL 4 probability of dangerous failure on demand (low demand): 1e-05-0.0001 PFD; PFD 0.0001-0.00001, RRF 10,000-100,000, low demand operation per IEC EN 61508. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_Integrity_Level)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://pdfcoffee.com/safety-design-and-api-rp-14c-2-pdf-free.html)
- Reliability — calibration/verification interval: 1 year; Typical recommendation is yearly checks and verification; calibration verified using test separator data or sampling. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en/measurement-instrumentation/products/roxar-2600-multiphase-flow-meter)
- Reliability — moving parts: 0 count; Roxar 2600 has no moving parts, reducing wear and maintenance needs and the need for spare parts. [source](https://www.emerson.com/en/measurement-instrumentation/products/roxar-2600-multiphase-flow-meter)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.oilgasz.com/2023/07/offshore-platform-wellhead-systems.html?m=1)
- Operational history — operational history: no range recorded; As printed on the cited page.. [source](https://www.slb.com/products-and-services/innovating-in-oil-and-gas/reservoir-characterization/reservoir-testing/surface-testing/surface-multiphase-flowmetering/vx-spectra-surface-multiphase-flowmeter)

## AI-assisted seismic interpretation and history matching
<a id="ups-ai-seismic-interpretation-history-matching"></a>

Machine-learning models, mostly convolutional neural networks, pick faults, horizons and geobodies, classify lithofacies and speed up denoising and velocity work, while automated and assisted history matching tunes reservoir models. A review of more than 500 SEG and EAGE papers (2010 to 2021) finds ML improves individual tasks but has not yet replaced whole workflows.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    TRAIN["Train on labelled seismic or simulation data<br/><small>training sets, GPUs</small>"]
    PRED["Predict faults, horizons or parameters<br/><small>trained network</small>"]
    QC["Interpreter review"]
    TRAIN --> PRED
    PRED --> QC
```

**Key equipment:** labelled data sets, GPU compute, interpretation platform.

**Performance:**
- structural interpretation time reduction with ML-assisted fault interpretation: 80 %; SLB Delfi AI success story: average 80% time-reduction in overall structural interpretation on a project; operator not named in the story. [source](https://www.slb.com/products-and-services/delivering-digital-at-scale/software/delfi/openness/ai-in-delfi/ai-success-stories)

**Reference plants:**
- Petrobras enterprise deployment of SLB Delfi digital platform, BR: enterprisewide 5-year contract covering exploration, development and production workflows, moving subsurface workflows to the cloud; operating. [source](https://jpt.spe.org/petrobras-picks-slbs-delfi-platform)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (seismic interpretation machine learning techno-economic cost per kilometer OR cost per square study); no open source states this. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (technology readiness level seismic interpretation OR history matching machine learning TRL assessment table); no TRL table found for this technology. Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://pubs.geoscienceworld.org/seg/geophysics/article/89/1/WA157/629087/Machine-learning-for-seismic-exploration-Where-are), [2](https://www.slb.com/products-and-services/delivering-digital-at-scale/software/delfi/openness/ai-in-delfi/ai-success-stories), [3](https://jpt.spe.org/petrobras-picks-slbs-delfi-platform), [4](https://www.cmgl.ca/resources/enhancing-co2-storage-confidence-through-spatial-history-matching-ai-driven-interpretation-and-dynamic-simulation-full). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## All-electric subsea systems
<a id="ups-all-electric-subsea"></a>

Electric actuators replace hydraulic fluid and its lines, so trees need only power and signals. OneSubsea won a contract for Equinor's Fram Sør, 12 all-electric trees on four templates tied back to Troll C, which Equinor calls the first large-scale all-electric subsea production system.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    PWR["Power and signals through electric umbilical<br/><small>electric umbilical</small>"]
    ACT["Electric actuators operate valves<br/><small>all-electric tree</small>"]
    PWR --> ACT
```

**Key equipment:** all-electric tree, electric actuators, electric umbilical, templates.

**Performance:**
- Fram Sør scope: 12 all-electric trees; four templates, tieback to Troll C. [source](https://www.oedigital.com/news/529280-equinor-picks-onesubsea-all-electric-subsea-production-system-for-north-sea-field)
- Fram Sør recoverable reserves: 116 million boe gross; 75% oil, 25% gas; PDO submitted 26 June 2025. [source](https://news.cision.com/var-energi-asa/r/development-plan-for-fram-sor-submitted,c4169146)
- Fram Sør investment: 21 NOK billion; more than NOK 21 billion (USD 2.2 billion) by the Fram partners; production scheduled to start at the end of 2029. [source](https://news.cision.com/var-energi-asa/r/development-plan-for-fram-sor-submitted,c4169146)

**Reference plants:**
- Equinor Fram Sør all-electric subsea development (North Sea, tieback to Troll C), NO: 116 mmboe gross recoverable; four templates with 12 all-electric trees (SLB OneSubsea); PDO submitted and development plan approved 26 June 2025; EPC contract for four templates and 12 all-electric trees awarded to SLB OneSubsea in August 2025; production scheduled to start at the end of 2029, so the first large-scale all-electric system is not yet operating (as of October 2026). [source](https://news.cision.com/var-energi-asa/r/development-plan-for-fram-sor-submitted,c4169146)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (all-electric subsea production system capex cost study; BSEE-hosted OTC 12941 lifecycle-cost paper checked); no open source gives costs specific to all-electric subsea systems. Where: arXiv and open-access journals: techno-economic or review papers (MDPI is blocked by its CDN; use the manual inbox) (catalog: arxiv, open); Licensor and EPC studies; paid cost databases are catalog-only (paid); OSTI.GOV full-text reports from DOE labs: search the technology name plus 'techno-economic' (catalog: osti, open). Next: Look for an open techno-economic study; record each figure with metric, range, unit, dollar year, basis and source.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. Web-checked 2026-10-28 (all-electric subsea technology readiness level TRL assessment); only paywalled SPE subsea-system readiness-level literature found, no open TRL table for all-electric subsea systems. Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://www.oedigital.com/news/529280-equinor-picks-onesubsea-all-electric-subsea-production-system-for-north-sea-field), [2](https://news.cision.com/var-energi-asa/r/development-plan-for-fram-sor-submitted,c4169146), [3](https://www.webwire.com/ViewPressRel.asp?aId=342967), [4](https://www.offshore-energy.biz/2026/10/22/onesubsea-and-oil-and-gas-giants-are-unlocking-deeper-waters-with-all-electric-subsea-tech/), [5](https://www.onesubsea.slb.com/solutions-and-capabilities/subsea-electrification). ## Downhole gas and water separation
<a id="ups-downhole-separation"></a>

Gravity or hydrocyclone separators split produced fluid downhole so the oil-rich stream is lifted and the water-rich stream is reinjected into another zone. Trials show properly operating systems may cut water brought to surface by about 75%.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IN["Packers isolate the zone and feed the intake<br/><small>packers</small>"]
    SEP["Hydrocyclone or gravity separation<br/><small>downhole hydrocyclone</small>"]
    OUT["Lift oil; reinject water<br/><small>ESP, injection zone</small>"]
    IN --> SEP
    SEP --> OUT
```

**Key equipment:** downhole hydrocyclone or gravity separator, ESP, packers.

**Performance:**
- water brought to surface reduced: 75 % (about); majority of properly operating DOWS in trials. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downhole_oil%E2%80%93water_separation_technology)
- downhole centrifugal separator efficiency: 80-100 %; Separation near 100% up to 500 bpd liquid rate, remaining above 80% at higher rates.. [source](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S1875510022000026)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Drilling automation and autonomous rigs
<a id="ups-drilling-automation"></a>

Software closes the loop between downhole sensing, rig control and well placement so the system adjusts weight on bit, rotation and hydraulics, and increasingly well trajectory, with limited human input. Halliburton reports 23.4% higher average ROP in three years of autonomous directional drilling on four Norwegian Continental Shelf rigs; these are company claims.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SENSE["Sense downhole and surface data<br/><small>sensors, MWD</small>"]
    DECIDE["Algorithm sets parameters or steers<br/><small>control software</small>"]
    ACT["Rig control executes and re-measures<br/><small>rig control system</small>"]
    SENSE --> DECIDE
    DECIDE --> ACT
```

**Key equipment:** rig control system, downhole sensors, automation software, remote operations centre.

**Performance:**
- average ROP, automated vs conventional (Halliburton NCS): 23.4 % improvement; 36.71 vs 29.75 m/h over three years, four rigs; vendor figure. [source](https://www.aveva.com/en/our-industrial-life/type/article/when-the-rig-starts-thinking-the-rise-of-autonomous-drilling/)
- autonomous share of operations in Guyana well: 93 % (over); SLB statement relayed by AVEVA. [source](https://www.aveva.com/en/our-industrial-life/type/article/when-the-rig-starts-thinking-the-rise-of-autonomous-drilling/)
- ROP gain in multi-basin closed-loop study: 1-58 % (laterals 1 to 22, intermediate up to 58); 100 wells, Bakken, Delaware, Eagle Ford. [source](https://www.gadrilling.com/glossary/autonomous-drilling)
- drilling cost reduction from automation and digitalisation: 10-20 %; Rystad estimated 10-20% lower drilling costs and 10-30% lower facility and subsea costs.. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/field-development/article/14068181/digitalization-automation-could-save-upstream-market-100-billion-rystad-energy-finds)

**Reference plants:**
- Nabors R801 robotic drilling rig (with ExxonMobil), US: world's first fully automated land drilling rig; purpose-built proving ground for drilling automation; operating. [source](https://investor.nabors.com/2022-11-16-Nabors-Marks-Major-Industry-Milestone-Fully-Automates-Existing-Land-Rig-with-First-of-its-Kind-Robotics-Module)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Heavy oil upgrading (in-field)
<a id="ups-heavy-oil-upgrading-in-field"></a>

Upgrading is done near or in the reservoir to cut viscosity before transport. THAI's in-situ combustion upgraded oil by 3 to 7 API points at Kerrobert, and a nanocatalyst pilot in Colombia raised API from 11.6° to 13.4° while viscosity fell from 39,987 cP to 11,734 cP.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IN["Heat or catalyse heavy oil in place<br/><small>combustion front, catalyst</small>"]
    OUT["Produce partially upgraded oil"]
    IN --> OUT
```

**Key equipment:** air injection or catalyst delivery, horizontal producers.

**Performance:**
- THAI upgrading: 3-7 API points; Whitesands and Kerrobert pilots. [source](https://www.insitucombustion.ca/Advanced_THAI_Brochure.pdf)
- heavy crude definition: 20 ° API (below); extra heavy below 10° API. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_crude_oil)
- API gravity gain from CAPRI catalytic upgrading: 2-7 degrees API; Laboratory microreactor at 350-425 C and 20 barg for 9.2 min: 2-7 API gain and 42-82% viscosity reduction on a 13 API feed.. [source](https://pubs.acs.org/doi/10.1021/ie400661x)

**Reference plants:**
- Long Lake SAGD with in-field upgrader (Nexen, later CNOOC), Alberta, CA: design 72,000 bpd bitumen (SAGD + integrated in-field upgrader); operator guided 38,000-45,000 bpd for 2011; operating. [source](https://www.reuters.com/article/markets/nexen-says-long-lake-output-to-lag-design-idUSN16114618)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Searched OSTI corpus (25 reports) for "capital cost", "operating cost", "$/bbl", "petroleum", "resid"; not present; 2026-10-28 Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found (Long Lake capex figures are project-specific, not per-barrel upgrading economics). ## Oil shale retorting
<a id="ups-oil-shale-retorting"></a>

Oil shale is heated without oxygen to 450 to 500 °C so kerogen decomposes into oil, gas and residue. Only Kiviter, Galoter, Fushun and Petrosix remained in commercial use by 2006; Estonia produced 355,000 t of shale oil in 2008 and Brazil 200,000 t.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    MINE["Mine and crush shale<br/><small>mining equipment</small>"]
    RET["Retort at 450 to 500 °C<br/><small>retort</small>"]
    SEP["Condense oil; handle spent shale<br/><small>condenser</small>"]
    MINE --> RET
    RET --> SEP
```

**Key equipment:** retort, mining equipment, condensers, spent shale disposal.

**Performance:**
- retorting temperature: 450-500 °C; typical retorting temperature range as stated in the source. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale)
- energy return (EROI): 0.7-13.3 ratio; 1984 study across deposits; development projects claim 3 to 10. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale)
- production cost estimate: 70-95 USD/bbl (2005$); RAND 2005 estimate for the US. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale)

**Reference plants:**
- Enefit shale-oil plants (Eesti Energia), Estonia, EE: group shale-oil production 475,000 t in 2023 (including the Enefit 280-2 oil plant), up 11.9% on 2022; record 411,000 t in 2018; operating. [source](https://public-docs.energia.ee/ettevottest/investorile/2023/EE-aastaaruanne-2023-ENG.pdf)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found (Eesti Energia reports state production, not retorting economics). ## Subsea separation and water reinjection
<a id="ups-subsea-separation"></a>

A seabed separator splits water and sand from the flow; the water is reinjected, so the host handles only oil and gas. Equinor's Tordis system, planned when water reached 70 to 80% and sand 500 kg/day, was expected to raise recovery from 49% to 55% (about 35 million barrels).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    SEP["Gas-liquid-water-sand separation<br/><small>gravity separator, desander</small>"]
    BOOST["Boost oil to host and water to injection<br/><small>multiphase pump, water injection pump</small>"]
    INJ["Reinject water and sand<br/><small>injection well</small>"]
    SEP --> BOOST
    BOOST --> INJ
```

**Key equipment:** separator vessel (170 t), desander, two 2.3 MW pumps, manifold (230 t), foundation base.

**Performance:**
- Tordis expected recovery factor: 49-55 % (before to after); about 35 MMbbl extra. [source](https://www.offshore-mag.com/subsea/article/16760845/tordis-becomes-worlds-first-subsea-processing-installation)

**Reference plants:**
- Tordis subsea separation and water reinjection station, Tordis field, North Sea, NO: first commercial full-scale subsea separation installation: separator station at about 200 m water depth, separated water and sand to a dedicated injection well; online October 2007; pilot. [source](https://equinor.industriminne.no/en/seabed-separator)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Well plug and abandonment technologies (thermite, bismuth plugs)
<a id="ups-thermite-bismuth-plugs"></a>

Thermite heaters melt tubulars or a bismuth alloy so the metal re-solidifies as a gas-tight rock-to-rock barrier, run rigless on electric line. BiSN reports more than 500 deployments and 120 patents, including a 30-well Aker BP campaign; Interwell's RockSolid uses thermite for permanent abandonment, slot recovery and sustained-casing-pressure cures.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    RUN["Run tool on electric line<br/><small>wireline unit, thermite heater</small>"]
    MELT["Thermite reaction melts alloy or well elements<br/><small>bismuth alloy or thermite charge</small>"]
    SET["Alloy cools to a sealing plug; pressure test<br/><small>pressure test</small>"]
    RUN --> MELT
    MELT --> SET
```

**Key equipment:** thermite heater, bismuth alloy plug, wireline unit, pressure testing.

**Performance:**
- BiSN deployments: 500 (more than); company statement. [source](https://www.bisn.com/)
- casing and tubing sizes served: 3.5-30 in OD; BiSN. [source](https://www.bisn.com/)

**Costs:**
- P&A cost with an electrically run bismuth plug versus section milling and cement remediation: 50 % reduction in P&A cost; stated as halving the costs of P&A; deck undated, latest dated operation in it is January 2025; percentage only, no currency figure given. [source](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/3rmh154v/the-wells-decommissioning-collab-overview.pdf)

**Reference plants:**
- Wellstrom T1000 electric bismuth-plug trial, TAQA Cormorant North, UK North Sea, GB: 9-5/8 x 13-3/8 in bismuth plug set on a platform well; NZTC covered Wellstrom's cost, up-front testing and third-party costs; pilot. [source](https://www.nsta.co.uk/news/wells-decommissioning-collab-overview)

**Missing inputs:**
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://www.bisn.com/), [2](https://www.interwell.com/), [3](https://www.nstauthority.co.uk/media/3rmh154v/the-wells-decommissioning-collab-overview.pdf), [4](https://bisn.com/technology). Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Gas hydrate production
<a id="ups-gas-hydrate-production"></a>

Methane locked in ice-like hydrates is released by depressurisation, heating or CO2 exchange. Japan's JOGMEC produced gas offshore at Nankai Trough in March 2013 by depressurisation; Mallik (Canada) showed constant flow by lowering pressure in 2008; 1 m3 of hydrate releases about 164 m3 of methane.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DRILL["Drill into hydrate-bearing sediment<br/><small>well</small>"]
    DEP["Lower pressure to dissociate hydrate<br/><small>pumps</small>"]
    PROD["Produce gas and water; control sand<br/><small>sand control</small>"]
    DRILL --> DEP
    DEP --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** production well, downhole pumps, sand control, gas-water separator.

**Performance:**
- methane released per m3 of hydrate: 164 m3; plus 0.87 m3 fresh water. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_clathrate)
- Japan estimate of methane at Nankai: 1.1 trillion m3 (at least); Japanese government estimate quoted in the source. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_clathrate)
- Shenhu Area trial gas production, South China Sea (2017 and 2020 offshore production tests): 5000-28713 m3/day average (2017 trial; 2020 trial average over 30 days); 2017 trial: over 300,000 m3 gas in 60 days, average daily extraction above 5,000 m3 (SCIO, 9 Jul 2017). 2020 trial: 861,400 m3 (86.14x10^4 m3) over 30 days of continuous production from 1,225 m water depth (US DOE/NETL International Gas Hydrate R&D Activities Update, Dec 2020); 861400/30 = 28,713 m3/day average. [source](https://www.energy.gov/sites/prod/files/2020/12/f81/International%20Activities%20Update.pdf)

**Costs:**
- unit cost of methane exploitation with the proposed reservoir-reconstruction method (simulation study): 0.46 USD/m3 gas (2025$); dollar year not stated (publication 2025). Process-simulation study by China University of Petroleum (East China) and National University of Singapore (Baojiang Sun, Praveen Linga): the proposed method raises gas production about 73% vs conventional depressurization and could cut exploitation cost to as low as 0.46 USD/m3, dropping to about 0.14 USD/m3 under optimal porosity/saturation conditions; simulation result, capital cost not stated. [source](https://www.eurekalert.org/news-releases/1103102)

**Missing inputs:**
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open). Next: Find a TRL table that lists this technology, or leave maturity resting on the evidence on the card.

Sources: [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_clathrate), [2](https://www.eurekalert.org/news-releases/1103102), [3](http://english.scio.gov.cn/2017-07/09/content_41185306.htm). Last reviewed 2026-10-29.

## In-situ combustion and solvent-assisted SAGD
<a id="ups-in-situ-combustion-solvent-sagd"></a>

Air injection burns part of the oil in the reservoir to generate heat; THAI (toe-to-heel air injection) pairs a vertical air injector with a horizontal producer. Solvent-assisted variants (VAPEX, eMSAGP, LASER) add vaporised solvent or liquid to steam. Romania's Suplacu de Barcau field has run in-situ combustion since 1970 to 1971 with over 55% recovery.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    IGN["Ignite oil layer with air injection<br/><small>air compressors, igniters</small>"]
    BURN["Combustion front moves toe to heel<br/><small>vertical injector, horizontal producer</small>"]
    PROD["Produce upgraded oil<br/><small>horizontal producer</small>"]
    IGN --> BURN
    BURN --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** air compressors, injection and horizontal production wells, gas handling.

**Performance:**
- Suplacu de Barcau oil recovery factor: 50.8-55 %; over 55% in abstract; 50.8% in the 2015 status table; field API gravity 16°. [source](https://www.matec-conferences.org/articles/matecconf/pdf/2021/12/matecconf_mse21_09009.pdf)
- THAI partial upgrading, Whitesands and Kerrobert pilots: 3-7 API points; Wolf Lake lab test 4 to 5 points. [source](https://www.insitucombustion.ca/Advanced_THAI_Brochure.pdf)
- Kerrobert per-well oil rate: 10-30 m3/day; 63 to 190 bbl/d; started 2009, 12 well pairs from 2011. [source](https://www.insitucombustion.ca/Advanced_THAI_Brochure.pdf)
- THAI field recovery factor after 10 years: 2.5 % of OOIP; About 2.5% total field oil recovery after 10 years at Kerrobert, with 10-30 m3/day oil per well.. [source](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0920410519311258)

**Reference plants:**
- Kerrobert THAI in-situ combustion pilot (Petrobank, later Baytex), Saskatchewan, CA: THAI (toe-to-heel air injection) pilot; per-well production and injection data published by the operator; numeric capacity not stated on the public data page; pilot. [source](https://petrobank.ca/sf/thai/thai.htm)

**Missing inputs:**
- capital cost and operating or unit cost, with dollar year and basis (costs): No open source read so far gives costs for this technology. Web-checked 2026-10-28; no open techno-economic study found. ## Microbial EOR
<a id="ups-microbial-eor"></a>

Microbes or their nutrients are injected to lower interfacial tension, reduce viscosity, plug thief zones or generate gas. A 1995 survey of 322 US projects found 81% raised production; the Alton field (Australia) reported a 40% increase in 12 months; cost is cited below $10 per incremental barrel.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    INJ["Inject microbes or nutrients<br/><small>injection skid</small>"]
    INC["Shut in to let microbes act"]
    PROD["Resume production and monitor"]
    INJ --> INC
    INC --> PROD
```

**Key equipment:** nutrient and microbe injection skid, monitoring laboratory.

**Performance:**
- projects with higher production (1995 survey): 81 % of 322 US projects; no project reduced production. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microbial_enhanced_oil_recovery)
- incremental cost: 10 USD per barrel (under); dollar year not stated. [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microbial_enhanced_oil_recovery)
- incremental oil from MEOR field treatments (Vizcacheras, Argentina; Carter sandstone, Alabama US; China blocks): 37000-69000 bbl incremental oil per project; Vizcacheras field (Papagayos reservoir, Argentina): 37,000 bbl additional oil Aug 2000-Aug 2003; Carter sandstone field (Alabama, US, nutrient injection): 69,000 bbl extra oil (project reported around yearend 1996); China blocks reported in tonnes (Dagang Guan 69 about 8,700 t by Jul 2004; Daqing Chaoyanggou 9,175 t). Oil & Gas Journal review of 10 field cases, 221 wells. [source](https://www.ogj.com/home/article/17222121/meor-applications-conclusion-field-cases-illustrate-meor-effectiveness)

**Costs:**
- MEOR treatment cost per incremental barrel (Argentina field projects): 2.9-5.1 USD/bbl incremental oil (2009$); dollar year not stated (publication 2009). Vizcacheras field: "The estimated cost per incremental barrel was $2.90"; Piedras Coloradas field (six producers, Barrancas and Blanco formations): $5.10/bbl. [source](https://www.ogj.com/home/article/17222121/meor-applications-conclusion-field-cases-illustrate-meor-effectiveness)

**Missing inputs:**
- named plants or projects with capacity and year (reference_plants): The sources read name no specific plant. Where: Licensor and operator press releases for the suppliers listed (open). Next: Add one or two plants with country, capacity and start-up year.

Last reviewed 2026-11-17.

## Nanofluid EOR
<a id="ups-nanofluid-eor"></a>

Nanoparticles (silica, zirconia, alumina) added to injection water alter rock wettability, lower interfacial tension and build a structural disjoining-pressure wedge. A 2025 review reports lab recoveries of 15 to 20% extra, an optimum concentration of 0.1 to 0.5 wt%, and only a few field trials (a Colombian heavy-oil pilot and Ghawar).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    DISP["Disperse stabilised nanoparticles in brine<br/><small>mixing skid</small>"]
    INJ["Inject as a slug<br/><small>injection pumps</small>"]
    DISP --> INJ
```

**Key equipment:** nanoparticle dispersion unit, injection pumps.

**Performance:**
- wettability contact-angle reduction: 58.8 % (up to); review of lab studies. [source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s13202-025-01965-1)
- optimum nanoparticle concentration: 0.1-0.5 wt%; body text gives 0.05 to 0.5 wt%. [source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s13202-025-01965-1)
- Colombian heavy-oil pilot cumulative production: 15502-30682 bbl; before to after; viscosity 39,987 to 11,734 cP. [source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s13202-025-01965-1)
- first field application of nanofluids in thermal EOR (cyclic steam stimulation), two Colombian wells: 3213-11966 bbl incremental oil per well (11,966 well A; 3,213 well B); average production increase 97%; functionalized-nanoparticle nanofluid, in-situ upgrading of heavy oil under CSS: post-treatment API gravity 11.6 to 13.4 deg and viscosity 39,987 to 11,734 cP in field; laboratory displacement tests raised recovery from 56% to 76% (lab viscosity to 104 cP, API to 29.2 deg); "production increases averaging 97%". [source](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0016236124028850)

**Costs:**
- operational cost reduction in the Colombian nanofluid CSS field trial: 48 % reduction vs conventional steam operations; "a 48% reduction in operational costs, primarily due to decreased steam generation and lower CO2 emissions" (field trial result, relative figure; no absolute capital or unit cost stated). [source](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0016236124028850)

**Reference plants:**
- Two-well nanofluid CSS field trial, heavy-oil field, Colombia, CO: cyclic steam stimulation in two wells; incremental 11,966 bbl (well A) and 3,213 bbl (well B); well names confidential in the source; pilot. [source](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0016236124028850)

**Missing inputs:**
- TRL with a cited source (trl): The IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide has no matching entry. no open TRL source found (IEA ETP guide checked 2026-10-28; no SETIS/DOE entry). Where: IEA ETP Clean Energy Technology Guide (collected): search for the technology name (catalog: iea-reports, open).
